{"parentUuid":"8ca0d311-8ed4-4e78-9cf5-12a9606fba48","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a5ea667e8aa34be02","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01MXpXGtBBjaqk9FL9tFw2zA","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Prohibited patterns in manuscript prose\n2\t\n3\t**Shared reference. Read on drafting, and read again on review.** The `paper-authoring`\n4\tskill and the `prose-reviewer` agent both consume this file, so a rule cannot mean one\n5\tthing while writing and another while reviewing.\n6\t\n7\t⚠️ **This is the shared FLOOR, not the whole review.** The reviewer additionally carries\n8\treader-outcome questions that do not appear here and that an author must never be given as\n9\ta checklist: a generator optimises against any list it can see. See the skill's §\"Why the\n10\treviewer knows things this file does not\".\n11\t\n12\tEvery rule below was derived from a measured sweep of the 21-bundle corpus, not from a\n13\tstyle guide. Where a number appears, it is what was counted.\n14\t\n15\t---\n16\t\n17\t## 1. The em-dash: prohibited outright, including in headings\n18\t\n19\t**Never write `---` or a literal `—`.** Two forms exist and both are banned.\n20\t\n21\t**Why this one is absolute.** An em-dash reads as machine-authored to a 2026 reader and\n22\tcosts trust before the physics is evaluated. One is as disqualifying as forty, so there is\n23\tno acceptable density. Enforced by `validate.py --check bundle_prose_em_dash_free`, target\n24\tzero. Measured before the corpus was swept: **741 of them, and 0 of 21 bundles clean.**\n25\t\n26\t### ⚠️ The en-dash `--` is MANDATORY and must never be touched\n27\t\n28\t`--` (exactly two hyphens) is a different character doing a different job, and the corpus\n29\tcarries **1,121** of them:\n30\t\n31\t- compound eponyms: `Bose--Einstein`, `Bekenstein--Hawking`, `Schwinger--Keldysh`,\n32\t  `Kaul--Majumdar`, `Chowdhury--Hartnoll--Hebbar--Khondaker`, `SK--EFT`\n33\t- numeric ranges: `pp. 10--15`, `$2$--$3$ K`\n34\t\n35\t**An author over-correcting to \"no dashes at all\" breaks every compound name in the\n36\tprogram, and the check cannot see it** because it counts exactly three hyphens. This is the\n37\tsingle highest-cost mistake available here. One live line contained both:\n38\t`observables---also drives the SK--EFT`.\n39\t\n40\tA third variant exists in the wild: the literal Unicode en-dash `–` (U+2013), found as\n41\t`Kaul–Majumdar` and `Chowdhury–Hartnoll–Hebbar–Khondaker`. Write `--` instead; D3 once\n42\tspelled the same name both ways.\n43\t\n44\t### Replacing an em-dash is a REWRITE, not a substitution\n45\t\n46\tA mechanical swap produces comma splices. Identify the job the dash was doing, then use the\n47\tpunctuation that already exists for that job. Frequencies below are from six independent\n48\treadings of all 741 occurrences.\n49\t\n50\t| The dash was doing | Frequency | Write instead |\n51\t|---|---|---|\n52\t| Paired aside interrupting a clause | ~45–55% | parentheses; a comma pair when the aside is short and contains no commas of its own |\n53\t| Introducing an explanation, expansion or list | ~30–45% | a colon |\n54\t| Separating a label from its gloss in `\\item`, `\\subsection{}`, `\\paragraph{}` | ~15% | a colon, always |\n55\t| Contrastive \"X, not Y\" interjection | ~7% | a comma pair (parentheses would demote a correction to an aside) |\n56\t| Joining two independent clauses | ~3–15% | a semicolon, or a full stop and a new sentence |\n57\t| Trailing appositive glossing a number's significance | common in E1/E2 | a comma |\n58\t\n59\t### The four hard cases, each of which defeats a mechanical fix\n60\t\n61\t1. **The aside contains its own parenthetical or citation.** Converting the outer dash to\n62\t   parentheses yields stacked or nested parens that read as a stutter:\n63\t   `(\\lean{diamondDist_eq_choiSDP}) (the Watrous semidefinite program~\\cite{Watrous2018}) is proven`.\n64\t   **Merge the tag and the aside into ONE parenthetical** with an internal comma or\n65\t   semicolon. Do not nest.\n66\t2. **The aside contains a comma-separated list.** Comma-punctuating it makes the reader\n67\t   parse the inner items as continuing the outer sentence's list. Use parentheses.\n68\t3. **The sentence already has a colon later on.** Adding a second colon reads as a nested\n69\t   label. Restructure with a copula: `X is the headline theorem: Y`.\n70\t4. **Three levels of subordination.** An aside inside an aside inside a main clause is a\n71\t   sentence trying to be three sentences. Split it.\n72\t\n73\t### The generative habits behind them: prohibit these, not just the symbol\n74\t\n75\tAll six readers converged on the same root, so treat it as the rule that matters:\n76\t\n77\t> **Do not interrupt a main clause to insert a clarification.** Write the clarification as\n78\t> its own sentence, before or after.\n79\t\n80\tThe dominant shape was *claim, dash, restatement-with-a-twist, dash, continuation*: draft a\n81\tclean declarative, then, worried a reader will misattribute a convention or miss a caveat,\n82\tsplice the qualifier into the middle. It concentrated in exactly the paragraphs doing the\n83\theaviest epistemic hedging (novelty disclaimers, provenance caveats, convention-pinning),\n84\twhich is where this project's prose is densest.\n85\t\n86\tFour more shapes, each of which reliably produces an em-dash:\n87\t\n88\t- **Name-then-gloss in itemised lists.** `\\item \\verb|foo_bar|: the substantive witness`.\n89\t  Use the colon from the start; this was the single most common pattern in the Lean-heavy\n90\t  bundles.\n91\t- **Label-then-gloss in headings.** `\\subsection{Pillar 1: Golterman--Shamir}`. A dash in a\n92\t  heading also reads as a tic when scanning the table of contents.\n93\t- **Tag-stacking.** A claim ending in a `\\lean{...}` identifier in parens, then a dash\n94\t  glossing that identifier. This is an appositive that got dash-punctuated only because the\n95\t  sentence had already spent its parentheses.\n96\t- **Claim-then-immediately-re-scope.** `T_H \\approx 2.4$ K --- nine orders of magnitude\n97\t  above atomic BEC --- with adiabaticity...`. A defensible instinct for a rigorous program,\n98\t  executed with the wrong punctuation: pick the syntax that matches the qualifier's\n99\t  grammatical role.\n100\t\n101\t---\n102\t\n103\t## 2. A fix may not narrate itself\n104\t\n105\t**Never tell the reader what an earlier draft of this paper said, when it was corrected, or\n106\twhich review round caught it.** A referee has no access to that process and cannot act on\n107\tit; the text reads as a changelog pasted into a manuscript.\n108\t\n109\tEnforced by `validate.py --check bundle_reader_facing_voice`, target zero. Banned forms:\n110\t\n111\t- a correction stamped with a date: `(corrected 2026-08-01)`\n112\t- an account of an earlier draft: `three earlier drafts of this paragraph asserted...`\n113\t- a first-person superseded claim: `We previously shipped a theorem asserting...`\n114\t- an internal review reference: `(D11 Stage-13 round-14 finding 6.2)`\n115\t- the project's own planning documents: `Our earlier planning documents described...`\n116\t- review rounds cast as actors: `rounds 7 and 10 both rated this cosmetic`\n117\t- a claim about the paper's own drafting: `typeset unconditionally in every draft`\n118\t\n119\t**And never tell the reader that you did not read a source you cite.** Same rule, second\n120\tshape (added 2026-08-15): it is an account of your process, and it leaves the citation\n121\tstanding as support for a claim while the prose says nobody read it. Banned forms:\n122\t\n123\t- an unread cited source: `we have not inspected its text`\n124\t- a citation held as metadata: `we hold that source only as a resolved DOI record`\n125\t- a citation read off its abstract: `we have read \\cite{X} in abstract only`\n126\t\n127\t**The fix is to acquire the source, never to delete the sentence.** Dropping the hedge while\n128\tkeeping the citation converts a visible gap into an invisible one (ADR-014). If the source\n129\tcannot be acquired, the claim must stand without it or not stand.\n130\t\n131\t⚠️ **Three shapes look alike and only one is prohibited.** Keep the other two:\n132\t\n133\t- ✓ **the cited source is itself preliminary** — *\"presents itself as an ongoing project…so\n134\t  we do not read it as establishing how much of that layer is finished\"*. You are\n135\t  characterising the work's maturity, not confessing that you skipped it.\n136\t- ✓ **a novelty search states its scope** — *\"HOL Light was not among the ecosystems we\n137\t  assessed and we do not assert absence there\"*. This is REQUIRED on an absence claim.\n138\t\n139\tThe line is the noun: not reading *its text* is prohibited; not having surveyed *that\n140\tdevelopment* is the scope statement a priority claim owes its reader.\n141\t\n142\t**The history is not lost, it moves.** `papers/<X>/change_log.md` and\n143\t`docs/review_finding_supersessions.json` are where a later reader can actually check it, and\n144\tthe ledger entry is required by the lift procedure anyway.\n145\t\n146\t### ⚠️ Removing narration is not deleting content\n147\t\n148\tMost of these passages wrap something real, and the substance must survive:\n149\t\n150\t- **A retraction is a scientific disclosure.** Restate it as a present-tense negative\n151\t  result: what is *not* claimed and why it fails. Do not delete the paragraph.\n152\t- **A scope correction states the correct scope.** Keep every factual statement about what\n153\t  a module does and does not contain, including file:line references. Drop only \"earlier\n154\t  drafts called it X\" and the date/round/finding tag.\n155\t- **A completeness disclaimer is a real caveat.** Keep it; drop the account of what two\n156\t  earlier drafts asserted.\n157\t\n158\t### What is NOT prohibited\n159\t\n160\tReporting that *the literature* corrected something is ordinary scholarship: `Smith\n161\tcorrected this coefficient in 2019` is correct and stays.\n162\t\n163\tDescribing the verification pipeline is legitimate **when the pipeline is the paper's\n164\tsubject matter** (I1, and I2/I3 for library conventions). Addressing the paper's own\n165\treferees is legitimate anywhere: `lets Mathlib4 reviewers calibrate expectations`.\n166\t\n167\tThis distinction is why the rule targets the *act* of self-narration rather than a\n168\tvocabulary. A word list banning `Stage 13`, `reviewer` and `adversarial review` scores 90\n169\thits on this corpus, of which 48 are legitimate uses in the methodology paper alone.\n170\t\n171\t---\n172\t\n173\t## 3. Claim discipline\n174\t\n175\tThese are enforced elsewhere in the pipeline; they are repeated here because they are\n176\tcheaper to satisfy while drafting than to repair at review.\n177\t\n178\t- **Never hedge a count or numerical literal with \"alt convention\" or \"+N depending on\n179\t  convention\" without proving the convention sensitivity.** If counts disagree across\n180\t  tools, find the truth. Convention-drift framing masks actual content drift: a \"161 ± 3\n181\t  alt convention\" was really 164, and the +3 was real drift.\n182\t- **Verify which registry a name lives in before citing it.** `AXIOM_METADATA`,\n183\t  `PLACEHOLDER_THEOREMS`, `HYPOTHESIS_REGISTRY` and `BORDISM_HYPOTHESES` are four different\n184\t  tables; a name found in one and attributed to another is a Stage-13 blocker.\n185\t- **Every cross-language prior-art claim must resolve to a verifiable URL or canonical\n186\t  ecosystem reference.** Hallucinated libraries have shipped before. When in doubt, cite\n187\t  the ecosystem (UniMath, Mathlib4) rather than naming a specific library.\n188\t- **When broadening an attribution, verify every addition belongs in the broadened\n189\t  category.** Surface code is abelian-stabiliser QEC, not non-Abelian-anyon TQC.\n190\t- **Numbers come from the pipeline.** Use `\\input{tables/<spec>.tex}` and the `counts.tex`\n191\t  macros rather than typing a literal.\n192\t\n193\t---\n194\t\n195\t## 4. Structure\n196\t\n197\t- **The abstract leads with the result**, not with the programme, the architecture, or what\n198\t  the paper will do.\n199\t- **The reader never sees the project's internal taxonomy.** Not `bundle`, `tier`, `wave`,\n200\t  `phase`, or `splash`. Companion papers are cited as papers, by title, in a normal\n201\t  reference list. This is what makes a manuscript read as a repository index: F's organising\n202\t  vocabulary was its own publication plan.\n203\t- **Section architecture is authored, not accumulated.** A bundle's sections come from its\n204\t  charter, written before any source is registered. Do not add a section because a source\n205\t  was registered: D3 has 37 sections against 31 sources, which is sedimentation.\n206\t- **Every figure is planned.** A bundle with no figures is a charter defect, not a style\n207\t  choice; 9 of 21 bundles shipped zero figures because the lift step only ever *migrated*\n208\t  figures that already existed.\n209\t"}]},"uuid":"bc28a6ab-7384-47de-88fd-e71042698d52","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:37:55.439Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"8ca0d311-8ed4-4e78-9cf5-12a9606fba48","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"3c5c636d-e4a1-4d20-af0f-eed6861293dc","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a5ea667e8aa34be02","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XhEbtgRpAWvurC57exd1jc","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# D2 outline v2 — \"Anomaly constraints on Standard-Model particle content\"\n2\t\n3\tVenue: PRD | JHEP. Length target 18–45pp. Audience: high-energy theory, particle\n4\tphysics, lattice QFT, mathematical physics.\n5\t\n6\t## THE SPINE (v2 — v1's spine was an overclaim and was withdrawn)\n7\t\n8\tTwo headline claims, both falsifiable, neither a methodology claim:\n9\t\n10\t**(I)** Within Wang's modular proposal together with the Dai–Freed ℤ₁₆ classification,\n11\tthe Standard Model *without* right-handed neutrinos is excluded at the observed\n12\tN_f = 3, by two obstructions that see disjoint data: a fractional chiral central\n13\tcharge c₋ = 45/2, and a ℤ₁₆ residue of −3. The framework therefore requires ν_R\n14\tor a compensating sector.\n15\t\n16\t**(II)** Separately and unconditionally, 8 divides the signature of every even\n17\tunimodular form, kernel-checked by a route that does not pass through its own\n18\tconclusion; and the residual factor of two is provably **not** the lattice Arf\n19\tinvariant.\n20\t\n21\t### What is NOT claimed (and was claimed in the previous draft)\n22\t\n23\tThe ℤ₁₆ argument does **not** constrain the generation count.\n24\t- With ν_R: `16·N_f ≡ 0 (mod 16)` for every N_f. No constraint on the count.\n25\t- Without ν_R: `15·N_f ≡ 0 (mod 16)` with `gcd(15,16) = 1`, so `N_f ∈ 16ℤ`, which\n26\t  *excludes* N_f = 3.\n27\tThe generation constraint `N_f ∈ 3ℤ` comes from **one** argument (modular), and is\n28\tconditional on Wang's proposal. The composed no-ν_R condition is `N_f ∈ 48ℤ`, an\n29\texclusion, not a second derivation.\n30\t\n31\t## Section order\n32\t\n33\t- **§1 Introduction.** Result first. A forward-facing table of assumptions A1–A5 with\n34\t  a claim attached: the chain from Weyl count to N_f ∈ 3ℤ rests on exactly these, each\n35\t  is either an established result absent from Mathlib or a proposal of the cited\n36\t  author's, and the two are distinguished. Falsifier for each headline claim.\n37\t- **§2 Setup.** Spin^{ℤ₄} structure and why the SM admits one; what a Dai–Freed anomaly\n38\t  asserts operationally; which boundary theory carries c₋. Physics, not citation.\n39\t- **§3 The generation constraint.** c₋ = 8N_f; Wang's five-step chain disclosed AS HIS\n40\t  PROPOSAL; the machine-checked arithmetic core. Ext^n_{A(1)} through degree 5 folded\n41\t  in, with H2 stated as an assumption in the sentence that consumes it. Schellekens\n42\t  demoted to one paragraph.\n43\t- **§4 The ν_R constraint.** 15 vs 16 components; 45 ≡ −3 (mod 16) as arithmetic, NOT\n44\t  attributed to a Lean theorem; the 48ℤ composition stated as the exclusion it is; what\n45\t  the compensating sector can and cannot be.\n46\t- **§5 Signatures of even unimodular forms.** The unconditional theorem; the\n47\t  classification route as its proof with rank 4 argued in full; the residual topological\n48\t  factor; the Arf no-go as a numbered THEOREM, not a caveat.\n49\t- **§6 Lattice realization.** Compressed. Whether these algebraic constraints admit a\n50\t  lattice realization; the Golterman–Shamir applicability scope, the Gioia–Thorngren\n51\t  positive construction, and the 4+1D conjecture disclosed as OPEN up front.\n52\t- **§7 Discussion and comparison with the literature on generation number.**\n53\t- **§8 Conclusions.**\n54\t\n55\tNo section titled \"Discharging hypothesis H2\". No terminal ledger section. No\n56\t\"four-thread convergence\" framing and no four-sixteens index as a result.\n57\t\n58\t## Figures\n59\t\n60\t1. `d2_fig1_generation_constraint.png` — c₋ = 8N_f against the 24-divisibility ladder.\n61\t2. `d2_fig2_fermion_z16_anomaly.png` — per-species components summing to 16, with the\n62\t   15 line.\n63\t3. `d2_fig3_modular_invariance_phase.png` — the T-phase against N_f.\n64\t(A §5 dependency figure is desirable; deferred if not drawable from existing functions.)\n65\t"}]},"uuid":"e88ad7cc-982e-4703-b459-d55b5cad6e00","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:37:56.465Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"3c5c636d-e4a1-4d20-af0f-eed6861293dc","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"4381a3bd-e4a7-40c1-b7ad-388f5b8cb1aa","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a5ea667e8aa34be02","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SWXxWncAi6VjUUybRMP7PE","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tname: paper-authoring\n3\tdescription: This skill should be used when the user asks to \"draft a paper section\", \"write the bundle draft\", \"author the manuscript\", \"lift content into a bundle\", \"write up this wave for publication\", \"draft the abstract\", \"revise the paper prose\", or when editing any `papers/<bundle>/paper_draft.tex`. Provides the house voice, the prohibited-pattern floor, and venue conventions for SK-EFT Hawking publication bundles.\n4\t---\n5\t\n6\t# Authoring a publication bundle\n7\t\n8\tGuidance for writing manuscript prose in `papers/<bundle>/paper_draft.tex`. This is the\n9\tgenerative counterpart to the `prose-reviewer` agent; both read the same prohibited-pattern\n10\treference so a rule cannot drift between writing and review.\n11\t\n12\tRead it either way you draft: the lead writing in-context, or a `paper-drafter` agent\n13\tdispatched over one section. `paper-drafter` reads this file and the same reference by\n14\tpath, so the floor is identical however the prose is produced.\n15\t\n16\t**Read `references/prohibited-patterns.md` before drafting.** It is mandatory, not\n17\tbackground, and it is short.\n18\t\n19\t---\n20\t\n21\t## Why this skill exists\n22\t\n23\tThe pipeline had three reviewer agents and no authoring guidance. Everything about how to\n24\twrite lived in `docs/BUNDLE_LIFT_PROCEDURE.md` §7 as a bookkeeping checklist consulted at\n25\tlift time. The measured result of that gap, across 21 bundles:\n26\t\n27\t- **741 em-dashes**, with zero bundles clean\n28\t- **13 passages** narrating the paper's own correction history to a referee\n29\t- **9 of 21 bundles shipping zero figures**, because the lift step only migrated figures\n30\t  that already existed and no step ever planned one\n31\t- **D3 carrying 37 sections against 31 contributing sources**, which is sedimentation\n32\t  rather than architecture\n33\t\n34\tNone of that is a capability limit. It is what an unguided generator produces when the only\n35\tcontrols are downstream reviewers.\n36\t\n37\t---\n38\t\n39\t## Before writing a section\n40\t\n41\t1. **Read the charter.** `papers/<X>/bundle_metadata.json` carries `target_journal` and\n42\t   `length_target`; the section belongs to a planned outline, not to a source paper that\n43\t   happened to be registered.\n44\t2. **Read the substrate directly.** Contributing per-paper drafts, the Lean modules, the\n45\t   roadmap. Do not write from a summary.\n46\t3. **Know the claim this section makes** and which apex theorem backs it. A section whose\n47\t   claim has no Lean witness is a finding waiting to happen.\n48\t\n49\t## While writing\n50\t\n51\tWrite for a referee at the named venue who has never seen the repository, cannot run the\n52\tcode, and will not read a second sentence to understand the first.\n53\t\n54\t- **State the result, then support it.** Not the programme, not the architecture, not what\n55\t  the section will do.\n56\t- **One clause, one job.** Do not interrupt a main clause to insert a clarification; write\n57\t  the clarification as its own sentence. This single habit produced most of the corpus's\n58\t  em-dashes.\n59\t- **No em-dash, ever** (`---` or `—`). **`--` is mandatory** for compound eponyms and\n60\t  ranges and must never be touched. Both rules and every replacement case are in\n61\t  `references/prohibited-patterns.md` §1; read them before reaching for a dash.\n62\t- **A fix never narrates itself.** Correct silently in the manuscript and record the history\n63\t  in `change_log.md` and the supersession ledger (§2 of the reference).\n64\t- **Numbers come from the pipeline** via `\\input{tables/<spec>.tex}` and the `counts.tex`\n65\t  macros, never typed as literals.\n66\t- **Cite companion work as papers**, by title. The reader never sees `bundle`, `tier`,\n67\t  `wave`, `phase` or `splash`.\n68\t\n69\t## Before handing off\n70\t\n71\t```bash\n72\tuv run python scripts/compile_bundle_pdf.py <X>   # must be clean\n73\tuv run python scripts/validate.py \\\n74\t    --check bundle_prose_em_dash_free \\\n75\t    --check bundle_reader_facing_voice \\\n76\t    --check bundle_manuscript_length\n77\t```\n78\t\n79\tCompile through `compile_bundle_pdf.py`, never `pdflatex` inside `papers/<X>/`. An in-place\n80\tcompile clobbers `paper_draft.{aux,log,synctex.gz}`, and a reviewer running concurrently then\n81\tjudges your draft by a `.log` that belongs to someone else's pass.\n82\t\n83\tAll three are deterministic and all three must pass. They are a floor, not a review: they\n84\tconfirm nothing prohibited is present, and say nothing about whether the argument carries.\n85\t\n86\t---\n87\t\n88\t## Why the reviewer knows things this file does not\n89\t\n90\tThe `prose-reviewer` agent reads this same reference, so the two never disagree about a\n91\trule. It also carries a brief that is deliberately **not** written down here, framed on\n92\treader outcome rather than rule compliance: where a reader would stop, whether the abstract\n93\tleads with the result, whether each section advances a single argument.\n94\t\n95\tThat asymmetry is intentional. A generator optimises against any checklist it can see, so a\n96\treviewer briefed entirely off the author's rules would pass anything the author produced.\n97\tSatisfying this file is necessary and not sufficient.\n98\t\n99\t---\n100\t\n101\t## Additional resources\n102\t\n103\t### Reference files\n104\t\n105\t- **`references/prohibited-patterns.md`** — mandatory read. The em-dash prohibition and its\n106\t  replacement cases, the self-narration ban, claim discipline, structure rules. Shared with\n107\t  the `prose-reviewer` agent.\n108\t\n109\t### Related surfaces\n110\t\n111\t- `docs/BUNDLE_LIFT_PROCEDURE.md` — the 14-step lift workflow this drafting sits inside\n112\t  (§7 authoring, §11 the BLOCKER loop, §12a the terminal de-scarring pass).\n113\t- `docs/WAVE_EXECUTION_PIPELINE.md` Stage 10 — the process law and its gates.\n114\t- `docs/BUNDLE_DIRECTORY_SCHEMA.md` — `length_target`, `apex_theorems`, and who writes what.\n115\t"}]},"uuid":"4ed26703-b5b1-415d-a679-6e155fdd93eb","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:38:01.589Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"4381a3bd-e4a7-40c1-b7ad-388f5b8cb1aa","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"945b1269-bf42-4dbe-b5f7-18c460c832af","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a5ea667e8aa34be02","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Xp9TMT7r8c1x2BKe9S4jPh","type":"tool_result","content":"270\t$24 \\mid 8 N_f \\Rightarrow 3 \\mid N_f$ was closed by the Aristotle\n271\tautomated prover (run id \\texttt{a1dfcbde}) and lives in\n272\t\\texttt{GenerationConstraint.generation\\_mod3\\_constraint}.\n273\t\n274\t\\subsection{Without $\\nu_R$: a fractional anomaly}\n275\t\n276\tEquation~\\eqref{eq:c-no-nuR} gives $c_{-} = 15/2$ for $N_f = 1$,\n277\twhich is fractional. Under the Wang hypothesis, the framing\n278\tconstraint $24 \\mid c_{-}$ then has \\emph{no} integer solution at\n279\tall, and gravity does not couple consistently to the fermion\n280\tcontent. This is captured at the Lean level in\n281\t\\texttt{WangBridge.central\\_charge\\_fractional\\_without\\_nu\\_R}.\n282\tA consistent SM extension thus requires either (a) right-handed\n283\tneutrinos or (b) a hidden Z$_{16}$-charged sector that supplies\n284\tthe missing single Weyl component (we develop the latter route in\n285\t\\S\\ref{sec:z16-drinfeld}). Note that this is an\n286\t\\emph{independent} argument for $\\nu_R$, complementary to the\n287\tgauge-anomaly cancellation argument routinely cited from\n288\tAlvarez-Gaum\\'e--Witten~\\cite{AlvarezGaumeWitten1984}: the\n289\tmodular framing constraint sees only the chiral central charge,\n290\tnot the gauge content.\n291\t\n292\t\\subsection{The 16-convergence and Rokhlin}\n293\t\n294\tThe integer $16$ first appears as the Weyl count above. Two\n295\tfurther appearances tighten the result. (a) On the $\\mathbb{Z}_{16}$\n296\tside, the Dai-Freed integer $\\eta_5^{{\\rm Spin}^{\\mathbb{Z}_4}}$\n297\ttakes values in $\\mathbb{Z}_{16}$~\\cite{DaiFreed1994}, and the\n298\t$\\nu_R$-completed SM saturates this exactly at one full\n299\tgeneration. (b) On the Rokhlin side, the signature of a closed\n300\tspin 4-manifold is divisible by 16~\\cite{Rokhlin1952}; the\n301\talgebraic-Rokhlin Lean module establishes the underlying\n302\t$8 \\mid \\sigma$ Serre bound on signatures of even unimodular forms\n303\tvia the $E_8$ Cartan matrix: \\texttt{E8Lattice.e8\\_det\\_one}\n304\tdischarges unimodularity ($\\det = 1$, via cofactor expansion against\n305\t\\texttt{CartanMatrix.E\\_8}), \\texttt{E8Lattice.e8\\_diagonal\\_all\\_two}\n306\t+ \\texttt{e8\\_diagonal\\_even} discharge the even-form property,\n307\tand the chain of leading-principal-minor positivity\n308\t(\\texttt{e8\\_minor\\_1} through the higher minors via Sylvester's\n309\tcriterion) establishes positive-definiteness and hence $\\sigma(E_8)\n310\t= 8$.  The Serre algebraic bound is then automatic ($8 \\mid 8$);\n311\t\\texttt{e8\\_sigma\\_not\\_div\\_16} records that $E_8$ exhibits the\n312\talgebraic floor without saturating the topological Rokhlin bound,\n313\tformalising Freedman's counterexample. \\texttt{E8Lattice.rokhlin\\_gap}\n314\tencodes the structural gap ($16 \\mid 2\\sigma(E_8)$, $16 \\nmid\n315\t\\sigma(E_8)$). The validity of the characteristic-square pairing on\n316\t$E_8$ itself is closed by\n317\t\\texttt{AlgebraicRokhlin.char\\_sq\\_valid\\_e8}.\n318\t\n319\tThe full chain is formally enumerated in\n320\t\\texttt{RokhlinBridge.sixteen\\_convergence\\_full} (per that theorem's\n321\tLean docstring: it indexes the four \\emph{independent} occurrences\n322\tof the integer 16 across SM Weyl count, $\\mathbb{Z}_{16}$ Dai--Freed,\n323\tRokhlin's signature theorem, and Kitaev's DIII period, without\n324\tasserting a formal common-origin theorem; the common-origin\n325\tmathematical content is the external result of\n326\tWang~\\cite{Wang2024}). The arithmetic identity\n327\t$\\operatorname{lcm}(16, 3) = 48$ is discharged at the Lean level by\n328\t\\texttt{RokhlinBridge.constraints\\_without\\_nu\\_R}; combined with\n329\tthe prose-level no-$\\nu_R$ argument, this forces $N_f \\in 48\n330\t\\mathbb{Z}$, in tension with the observed $N_f = 3$. The strict\n331\tstrengthening $16 \\nmid 8$ (i.e., that the joint Z16-plus-Rokhlin\n332\tbound exceeds the algebraic $8 \\mid \\sigma$ alone) is the content\n333\tof \\texttt{RokhlinBridge.rokhlin\\_strictly\\_stronger}.\n334\t\n335\t\\paragraph{From assumed hypothesis to a wired theorem (spectra-free route).}\n336\tRather than carry $16 \\mid \\sigma$ as an opaque hypothesis, we expose its\n337\tcontent through a narrow interface. The structure\n338\t\\texttt{SpinRokhlinInterface.SmoothSpinManifold4} records a closed smooth\n339\tspin 4-manifold's even-unimodular intersection form (even and unimodular by\n340\tWu's formula and Poincar\\'e duality) in the field \\texttt{even\\_unimod}, and\n341\tthe topological factor of two $2 \\mid \\sigma/8$ in the field \\texttt{topo}.\n342\tIts signature is the \\emph{genuine} signature of the intersection form,\n343\t$\\sigma := \\mathrm{latticeSig}\\,(\\text{form})$ (\\texttt{LatticeSignature.lean}),\n344\tnot a free parameter. From these,\n348\t\\texttt{SmoothSpinManifold4.rokhlin}~$:~16 \\mid \\sigma$ is a kernel-pure\n349\ttheorem (standard axioms only; no global Rokhlin hypothesis, no new\n350\t\\texttt{axiom}), obtained via\n351\t\\texttt{RokhlinClassification.sixteen\\_dvd\\_latticeSig\\_of\\_eight\\_dvd\\_of\\_topo}\n352\t(the composition $8\\mid\\mathrm{latticeSig}$ with $2\\mid\\mathrm{latticeSig}/8\n353\t\\Rightarrow 16\\mid\\mathrm{latticeSig}$, in turn\n354\t\\texttt{rokhlin\\_from\\_serre\\_plus\\_topology} on \\texttt{latticeSig}); the companion\n355\t\\texttt{sixteen\\_convergence\\_unconditional} reruns the four-16 enumeration\n356\twith the $16\\mid\\sigma$ conjunct now an interface theorem (conditional on\n357\tthe tracked \\texttt{topo} field $2\\mid\\sigma/8$) rather than a bare assumed\n358\tinput. (The theorem name flags the \\emph{unconditional} discharge of the\n359\talgebraic $8\\mid\\sigma$ floor; the $16$ leg remains conditioned on\n360\t$2\\mid\\sigma/8$, as it must be: $16\\mid\\sigma$ is false for general\n361\teven-unimodular forms.)\n362\tThe dependency graph routes\n363\t\\emph{even-unimodular} $+$ \\emph{van der Blij} $\\Rightarrow 8\\mid\\sigma$,\n364\ttogether with $2\\mid\\sigma/8 \\Rightarrow 16\\mid\\sigma$, and so does\n365\t\\emph{not} pass through Anderson--Brown--Peterson or Rokhlin's theorem\n366\titself (which is the conclusion), avoiding the circularity of the\n367\t$\\Omega^{\\mathrm{Spin}}_4 \\cong \\mathbb{Z}$ bordism alternative.\n368\tThis isolates the irreducible content exactly as Finashin--Kharlamov--Viro\n369\tdescribe. The algebraic half $8\\mid\\sigma$ is van der Blij's lemma on even\n370\tunimodular forms; its zero-axiom discharge is completed through the\n371\t\\emph{classification of even unimodular lattices} ($L\\cong E_8^{\\,a}\\oplus\n372\t(-E_8)^{\\,b}\\oplus H^{\\,c}$, whence $\\sigma=8(a-b)$). The \\emph{signature\n373\tcalculus} for that route is machine-checked and complete (all kernel-pure;\n374\t\\texttt{ExtractDeps} baseline green): the signature is grounded in Mathlib as\n375\t$\\mathrm{latticeSig}\\,G=\\mathrm{sig}^{+}-\\mathrm{sig}^{-}$ of the real\n376\tquadratic form (\\texttt{LatticeSignature.lean}); the generators are evaluated\n377\t($\\sigma(E_8)=8$ via an integer-Cholesky positive-definiteness certificate\n378\t$C^{\\mathsf T}C=4\\,E_8$ decidable over $\\mathbb{Z}$, $\\sigma(-E_8)=-8$,\n379\t$\\sigma(H)=0$; \\texttt{E8Signature.lean}, \\texttt{LatticeSignatureCongr.lean});\n380\tcongruence invariance (Sylvester, \\texttt{latticeSig\\_congr}), block-sum\n381\tadditivity $\\sigma(A\\oplus B)=\\sigma(A)+\\sigma(B)$ for nondegenerate blocks\n382\t(\\texttt{BlockSignature.lean}, \\texttt{GeneratorNondeg.lean}), and the\n383\tindex-general signature with reindexing invariance (\\texttt{LatticeSigBlock.lean})\n384\tare all established, so any normal form yields $\\mathrm{latticeSig}=8(a-b)$ and\n385\thence $8\\mid\\sigma$ (\\texttt{RokhlinClassification.lean}). The classification's\n386\ttwo classical inputs are now \\emph{both} discharged as kernel-pure theorems,\n387\twith no remaining Mathlib gap. {"parentUuid":"6cebafa2-4c4d-44d1-8a38-c972ec6a5842","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a5ea667e8aa34be02","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_017FsxDJRGYPxt4GFGSTeRed","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/-\n2\t# Rank-4 Hasse–Minkowski: local isotropy of even unimodular forms\n3\t\n4\tThe weak [HM] hypothesis (`RokhlinFromHM.HasWeakIsotropicVectorHyp`) is discharged at rank ≥ 5\n5\t(`RokhlinHMDischarge.weakIsotropic_of_five_le`) and at ranks 1, 2, 3 (elementary / vacuous). The lone\n6\tremaining rank is **4**, the sub-frontier where local universality fails (anisotropic rank-4 forms exist\n7\tover each `ℚ_p`). For an *even unimodular* form the local inputs are nonetheless available:\n8\t\n9\t* **odd `p`** — the form is `ℤ_p`-unimodular, so its mod-`p` reduction is a nondegenerate rank-4 form over\n10\t  `𝔽_p`, isotropic by Chevalley–Warning (`finite_field_form_isotropic`); the isotropic point is automatically\n11\t  *simple* (nondegeneracy ⟹ nonzero gradient), so a single-variable Hensel lift (`hensels_lemma`) produces a\n12\t  `ℚ_p`-isotropic vector. This file builds that input (`evenUnimodular_isotropic_odd_padic`).\n13\t\n14\tThe reusable engine is `mulVec_update_coord_quadratic`: varying one coordinate `j` of a vector turns the\n15\tquadratic form into a univariate quadratic `A j j · t² + 2·(A *ᵥ y) j · t + y ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ y`, which is the\n16\tpolynomial Hensel acts on.\n17\t\n18\tKernel-pure, no axioms.\n19\t-/\n20\t\n21\timport Mathlib\n22\timport SKEFTHawking.AlgebraicRokhlin\n23\timport SKEFTHawking.HasseMinkowskiLocal\n24\timport SKEFTHawking.PadicSquare\n25\timport SKEFTHawking.HilbertProductFormula\n26\timport SKEFTHawking.RokhlinHMDischarge\n27\timport SKEFTHawking.RokhlinFromHM\n28\t\n29\tnamespace SKEFTHawking\n30\t\n31\topen scoped BigOperators\n32\topen Matrix QuadraticForm\n33\t\n34\t/-- **One-coordinate variation of a quadratic form is a univariate quadratic.** For a symmetric matrix\n35\t`A`, replacing coordinate `j` of `y` by `t` (where `y j = 0`) gives\n36\t`(y + t·e_j) ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ (y + t·e_j) = A j j · t² + 2·(A *ᵥ y) j · t + y ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ y`. The cross terms coincide\n37\tby symmetry of `A`. This is the polynomial Hensel's lemma acts on for the local lift. -/\n38\ttheorem mulVec_update_coord_quadratic {R : Type*} [CommRing R] {n : ℕ} (A : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) R)\n39\t    (hsymm : A.transpose = A) (y : Fin n → R) (j : Fin n) (t : R) :\n40\t    (y + t • (Pi.single j 1 : Fin n → R)) ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ (y + t • (Pi.single j 1 : Fin n → R))\n41\t      = A j j * t ^ 2 + 2 * (A *ᵥ y) j * t + y ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ y := by\n42\t  have hsingle : A *ᵥ (Pi.single j 1 : Fin n → R) = fun i => A i j := by\n43\t    ext i; rw [mulVec_single]; simp\n44\t  have hyAe : y ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ (Pi.single j 1 : Fin n → R) = (A *ᵥ y) j := by\n45\t    rw [hsingle]\n46\t    show ∑ i, y i * A i j = (A *ᵥ y) j\n47\t    rw [mulVec]\n48\t    show ∑ i, y i * A i j = ∑ i, A j i * y i\n49\t    refine Finset.sum_congr rfl fun i _ => ?_\n50\t    have hij : A i j = A j i := by\n51\t      have h := congrFun (congrFun hsymm j) i; rwa [Matrix.transpose_apply] at h\n52\t    rw [hij]; ring\n53\t  have heAe : (Pi.single j (1 : R)) ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ Pi.single j (1 : R) = A j j := by\n54\t    rw [hsingle, single_dotProduct]; simp\n55\t  have heAy : (Pi.single j (1 : R)) ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ y = (A *ᵥ y) j := by\n56\t    rw [single_dotProduct]; simp\n57\t  rw [mulVec_add, dotProduct_add, add_dotProduct, add_dotProduct,\n58\t    mulVec_smul, dotProduct_smul, dotProduct_smul, smul_dotProduct, smul_dotProduct,\n59\t    hyAe, heAe, heAy]\n60\t  simp only [smul_eq_mul]\n61\t  ring\n62\t\n63\topen Polynomial in\n64\t/-- **Odd-`p` local isotropy of a `ℤ_p`-unimodular form (rank ≥ 3).** A symmetric matrix over `ℤ_[p]`\n65\t(`p` odd) with unit determinant is isotropic over `ℤ_[p]` (hence `ℚ_[p]`). Its mod-`p` reduction is a\n66\tnondegenerate rank-≥3 form over `𝔽_p`, isotropic by Chevalley–Warning; the isotropic point is *simple*\n67\t(nondegeneracy ⟹ the gradient `Ā *ᵥ x̄` is nonzero), so a single-variable Hensel lift in the\n68\tgradient-nonzero coordinate produces an exact `ℤ_[p]` zero. -/\n69\ttheorem isotropic_padicInt_of_unit_det {p : ℕ} [Fact p.Prime] (hp : p ≠ 2) {n : ℕ} (hn : 3 ≤ n)\n70\t    (A : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℤ_[p]) (hsymm : A.transpose = A) (hdet : IsUnit A.det) :\n71\t    ∃ v : Fin n → ℤ_[p], v ≠ 0 ∧ v ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ v = 0 := by\n72\t  classical\n73\t  set f : ℤ_[p] →+* ZMod p := PadicInt.toZMod with hf\n74\t  set Abar : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) (ZMod p) := A.map f with hAbar\n75\t  -- mod-p reduction is nondegenerate\n76\t  haveI : NeZero p := ⟨(Fact.out : p.Prime).pos.ne'⟩\n77\t  have hdetbar : Abar.det ≠ 0 := by\n78\t    have hd : Abar.det = f A.det := by rw [hAbar]; exact (RingHom.map_det f A).symm\n79\t    rw [hd]; exact (hdet.map f).ne_zero\n80\t  -- the residue form is isotropic (Chevalley–Warning, rank ≥ 3)\n81\t  obtain ⟨xbar, hxbar0, hxbarz⟩ := finite_field_form_isotropic (K := ZMod p) hn Abar\n82\t  -- the isotropic point is simple: the gradient is nonzero\n83\t  have hgrad : Abar *ᵥ xbar ≠ 0 := by\n84\t    intro h\n85\t    exact hdetbar (Matrix.exists_mulVec_eq_zero_iff.mp ⟨xbar, hxbar0, h⟩)\n86\t  obtain ⟨j, hj⟩ : ∃ j, (Abar *ᵥ xbar) j ≠ 0 := by\n87\t    by_contra h\n88\t    simp only [not_exists, ne_eq, not_not] at h\n89\t    exact hgrad (funext h)\n90\t  -- lift the residue solution to ℤ_[p]\n91\t  have hlift : ∀ i, ∃ X : ℤ_[p], f X = xbar i := fun i => ZMod.ringHom_surjective f (xbar i)\n92\t  choose x0 hx0 using hlift\n93\t  have hx0fun : (fun k => f (x0 k)) = xbar := funext hx0\n94\t  -- map-commutes for the matrix-vector product\n95\t  have hmapmv : ∀ (w : Fin n → ℤ_[p]) (i : Fin n),\n96\t      f ((A *ᵥ w) i) = (Abar *ᵥ (fun k => f (w k))) i := by\n97\t    intro w i\n98\t    simp only [Matrix.mulVec, dotProduct, map_sum, map_mul, hAbar, Matrix.map_apply]\n99\t  -- zero out coordinate j\n100\t  set y : Fin n → ℤ_[p] := Function.update x0 j 0 with hy\n101\t  set a : ℤ_[p] := x0 j with ha\n102\t  have hrestore : x0 = y + a • (Pi.single j 1 : Fin n → ℤ_[p]) := by\n103\t    ext i; rcases eq_or_ne i j with rfl | hij\n104\t    · simp [hy, ha]\n105\t    · simp [hy, Function.update_of_ne hij, Pi.single_eq_of_ne hij]\n106\t  -- the Hensel polynomial\n107\t  set F : ℤ_[p][X] := C (A j j) * X ^ 2 + C (2 * (A *ᵥ y) j) * X + C (y ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ y) with hF\n108\t  have hFe : ∀ x : ℤ_[p], (aeval x) F = A j j * x ^ 2 + 2 * (A *ᵥ y) j * x + y ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ y := by\n109\t    intro x; simp [hF]\n110\t  have hFda : (aeval a) (derivative F) = 2 * A j j * a + 2 * (A *ᵥ y) j := by\n111\t    simp [hF, derivative_add, derivative_mul]; ring\n112\t  -- F at a evaluates to the full quadratic Q(x0)\n113\t  have hQx0 : (aeval a) F = x0 ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ x0 := by\n114\t    rw [hFe]\n115\t    have h := mulVec_update_coord_quadratic A hsymm y j a\n116\t    rw [← hrestore] at h; rw [h]\n117\t  -- F'(a) = 2·(A *ᵥ x0) j\n118\t  have hgradx0 : 2 * A j j * a + 2 * (A *ᵥ y) j = 2 * (A *ᵥ x0) j := by\n119\t    have : (A *ᵥ x0) j = (A *ᵥ y) j + a * A j j := by\n120\t      rw [hrestore]\n121\t      simp [Matrix.mulVec_add, Matrix.mulVec_smul, mulVec_single]\n122\t    rw [this]; ring\n123\t  -- residue facts\n124\t  have hmod0 : f (x0 ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ x0) = 0 := by\n125\t    have : f (x0 ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ x0) = xbar ⬝ᵥ Abar *ᵥ xbar := by\n126\t      simp only [dotProduct, map_sum, map_mul]\n127\t      refine Finset.sum_congr rfl fun i _ => ?_\n128\t      rw [hx0, hmapmv x0 i, hx0fun]\n129\t    rw [this, hxbarz]\n130\t  have h2 : f (2 : ℤ_[p]) ≠ 0 := by\n131\t    have he : f (2 : ℤ_[p]) = ((2 : ℕ) : ZMod p) := by\n132\t      rw [hf, show (2 : ℤ_[p]) = ((2 : ℕ) : ℤ_[p]) by norm_num, map_natCast]\n133\t    rw [he, Ne, CharP.cast_eq_zero_iff (ZMod p) p 2]\n134\t    intro hdvd\n135\t    exact hp ((Nat.prime_dvd_prime_iff_eq Fact.out Nat.prime_two).mp hdvd)\n136\t  have hderiv_ne : f ((aeval a) (derivative F)) ≠ 0 := by\n137\t    rw [hFda, hgradx0, map_mul, hmapmv x0 j, hx0fun]\n138\t    exact mul_ne_zero h2 hj\n139\t  -- Hensel lift\n140\t  have ha1 : ‖(aeval a) (derivative F)‖ = 1 := by\n141\t    rcases lt_or_eq_of_le (PadicInt.norm_le_one ((aeval a) (derivative F))) with h | h\n142\t    · exact absurd ((toZMod_eq_zero_iff_norm_lt_one _).mpr h) hderiv_ne\n143\t    · exact h\n144\t  have hnorm : ‖(aeval a) F‖ < ‖(aeval a) (derivative F)‖ ^ 2 := by\n145\t    rw [ha1, one_pow]\n146\t    rw [hQx0]\n147\t    exact (toZMod_eq_zero_iff_norm_lt_one _).mp hmod0\n148\t  obtain ⟨z, hz, _⟩ := hensels_lemma (F := F) (a := a) hnorm\n149\t  -- assemble the lifted isotropic vector\n150\t  refine ⟨y + z • (Pi.single j 1 : Fin n → ℤ_[p]), ?_, ?_⟩\n151\t  · -- nonzero\n152\t    intro hv0\n153\t    have hsupp : ∀ i, i ≠ j → xbar i = 0 := by\n154\t      intro i hij\n155\t      have hvi := congrFun hv0 i\n156\t      simp only [Pi.add_apply, Pi.smul_apply, Pi.single_eq_of_ne hij, smul_zero, add_zero,\n157\t        Pi.zero_apply, hy, Function.update_of_ne hij] at hvi\n158\t      rw [← hx0 i, hvi, map_zero]\n159\t    have hxbarj : xbar j ≠ 0 := by\n160\t      intro hjz\n161\t      refine hxbar0 (funext fun i => ?_)\n162\t      rcases eq_or_ne i j with rfl | h\n163\t      · exact hjz\n164\t      · exact hsupp i h\n165\t    have hgv : (Abar *ᵥ xbar) j = Abar j j * xbar j := by\n166\t      rw [Matrix.mulVec, dotProduct, Finset.sum_eq_single j]\n167\t      · intro b _ hb; rw [hsupp b hb, mul_zero]\n168\t      · intro h; exact absurd (Finset.mem_univ j) h\n169\t    have hquad : Abar j j * xbar j ^ 2 = 0 := by\n170\t      have := hxbarz\n171\t      rw [dotProduct, Finset.sum_eq_single j] at this\n172\t      · rw [hgv] at this; rw [← this]; ring\n173\t      · intro b _ hb; rw [hsupp b hb, zero_mul]\n174\t      · intro h; exact absurd (Finset.mem_univ j) h\n175\t    have hAbarjj : Abar j j = 0 := by\n176\t      rcases mul_eq_zero.mp hquad with h | h\n177\t      · exact h\n178\t      · exact absurd (pow_eq_zero_iff (by norm_num) |>.mp h) hxbarj\n179\t    rw [hgv, hAbarjj, zero_mul] at hj; exact hj rfl\n180\t  · -- isotropic\n181\t    have h := mulVec_update_coord_quadratic A hsymm y j z\n182\t    rw [h, ← hFe z]; exact hz\n183\t\n184\t/-- **Weighted sum of squares is the diagonal Gram form.** `∑ wᵢ xᵢ² = x ⬝ᵥ diag(w) *ᵥ x`. The bridge from\n185\tthe abstract diagonalization `QuadraticForm.equivalent_weightedSumSquares` to a concrete diagonal matrix,\n186\tso the discriminant `∏ wᵢ = det (diag w)` is exposed for the square-discriminant computation. -/\n187\ttheorem weightedSumSquares_eq_diagonal {K : Type*} [CommRing K] {n : ℕ} (w : Fin n → K) :\n188\t    QuadraticMap.weightedSumSquares K w = (Matrix.diagonal w).toQuadraticMap' := by\n189\t  ext x\n190\t  rw [QuadraticMap.weightedSumSquares_apply, toQuadraticMap'_apply, dotProduct]\n191\t  refine Finset.sum_congr rfl fun i _ => ?_\n192\t  simp only [Matrix.mulVec_diagonal, smul_eq_mul, sq]; ring\n193\t\n194\t/-- **`toMatrix'` inverts `toQuadraticMap'` on symmetric matrices.** Over ℚ, a symmetric Gram matrix is\n195\trecovered from its quadratic form. -/\n196\ttheorem toMatrix'_toQuadraticMap' {n : ℕ} (M : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℚ) (hM : M.IsSymm) :\n197\t    (M.toQuadraticMap').toMatrix' = M := by\n198\t  have hsymm : ∀ x y : Fin n → ℚ,\n199\t      (Matrix.toLinearMap₂' ℚ M) x y = (Matrix.toLinearMap₂' ℚ M) y x := by\n200\t    intro x y\n201\t    rw [Matrix.toLinearMap₂'_apply', Matrix.toLinearMap₂'_apply',\n202\t        Matrix.dotProduct_mulVec, dotProduct_comm, ← Matrix.mulVec_transpose, hM.eq]\n203\t  -- v4.32: `QuadraticMap.toMatrix'` / `Matrix.toQuadraticMap'` are deprecated *aliases*, invisible\n204\t  -- to both `rw` and `unfold` (no usable equation theorems). `show` bridges to the real\n205\t  -- names at default transparency; the real defs then unfold normally. The STATEMENT deliberately\n206\t  -- keeps the old API name — the tree is mid-rename and a partial propagation breaks the interface.\n207\t  show QuadraticForm.toMatrix' (Matrix.toQuadraticForm' M) = M\n208\t  unfold QuadraticForm.toMatrix' Matrix.toQuadraticForm'\n209\t  rw [QuadraticMap.associated_left_inverse (S := ℚ) hsymm, LinearMap.toMatrix'_toLinearMap₂']\n210\t\n211\t/-- **Explicit rational congruence to the diagonalization.** A symmetric Gram form over ℚ isometric to\n212\t`∑ wᵢ xᵢ²` is matrix-congruent to `diagonal w` via the (invertible) change-of-basis `P = toMatrix' e`.\n213\tExposing `P` lets the congruence cast to every completion `ℝ`/`ℚ_p` (feeding `matrix_isotropic_congr`). -/\n214\ttheorem congr_of_equiv_weighted {n : ℕ} (A : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℚ) (hA : A.IsSymm)\n215\t    {w : Fin n → ℚ} (hwe : A.toQuadraticMap'.Equivalent (QuadraticMap.weightedSumSquares ℚ w)) :\n216\t    ∃ P : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℚ, IsUnit P.det ∧ A = Pᵀ * Matrix.diagonal w * P := by\n217\t  obtain ⟨e⟩ := hwe\n218\t  refine ⟨LinearMap.toMatrix' e.toLinearEquiv.toLinearMap, ?_, ?_⟩\n219\t  · have hmul : (LinearMap.toMatrix' e.toLinearEquiv.toLinearMap).det\n220\t        * (LinearMap.toMatrix' e.toLinearEquiv.symm.toLinearMap).det = 1 := by\n221\t      rw [← Matrix.det_mul, ← LinearMap.toMatrix'_comp,\n222\t          show e.toLinearEquiv.toLinearMap ∘ₗ e.toLinearEquiv.symm.toLinearMap = LinearMap.id from by\n223\t            ext x; simp, LinearMap.toMatrix'_id, Matrix.det_one]\n224\t    exact isUnit_iff_ne_zero.mpr (left_ne_zero_of_mul_eq_one hmul)\n225\t    -- v4.32 dot-notation fork: `X.toMatrix'` resolves to the *real* `QuadraticForm.toMatrix'` when\n226\t    -- `X`'s type head is `QuadraticForm`, but to the deprecated alias `QuadraticForm.QuadraticMap.\n227\t    -- toMatrix'` when it is `QuadraticMap` (as for `weightedSumSquares`). The two are defeq but not\n228\t    -- syntactically equal, so `rw` misses a visibly-present pattern. Name the real def explicitly.\n229\t  · have hWM : QuadraticForm.toMatrix' (QuadraticMap.weightedSumSquares ℚ w) = Matrix.diagonal w := by\n230\t      rw [weightedSumSquares_eq_diagonal, toMatrix'_toQuadraticMap' _ (Matrix.isSymm_diagonal w)]\n231\t    have hcomp : A.toQuadraticMap'\n232\t        = (QuadraticMap.weightedSumSquares ℚ w).comp e.toLinearEquiv.toLinearMap := by\n233\t      ext v; rw [QuadraticMap.comp_apply]; exact (e.map_app v).symm\n234\t    have h1 := congrArg QuadraticForm.toMatrix' hcomp\n235\t    rw [QuadraticForm.toMatrix'_comp, hWM, toMatrix'_toQuadraticMap' A hA] at h1\n236\t    exact h1\n237\t\n238\t/-- **`IsSquare (∏ wᵢ)` from `det A = 1`.** If a symmetric `det = 1` Gram form is isometric to the diagonal\n239\tform `∑ wᵢ xᵢ²`, then `∏ wᵢ` is a square: `det A = (det P)² · ∏ wᵢ`, so `∏ wᵢ = (det P)⁻²`. This pins the\n240\tdiagonalization's discriminant to a square — the square-discriminant hypothesis brick (b) consumes. -/\n241\ttheorem isSquare_prod_weights {n : ℕ} (A : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℚ) (hA : A.IsSymm)\n242\t    (hdet : A.det = 1) {w : Fin n → ℚ}\n243\t    (hwe : A.toQuadraticMap'.Equivalent (QuadraticMap.weightedSumSquares ℚ w)) :\n244\t    IsSquare (∏ i, w i) := by\n245\t  obtain ⟨P, hPunit, hAeq⟩ := congr_of_equiv_weighted A hA hwe\n246\t  have key : A.det = P.det ^ 2 * ∏ i, w i := by\n247\t    rw [hAeq, Matrix.det_mul, Matrix.det_mul, Matrix.det_transpose, Matrix.det_diagonal]; ring\n248\t  rw [hdet] at key\n249\t  have hne : P.det ≠ 0 := hPunit.ne_zero\n250\t  exact ⟨P.det⁻¹, by field_simp; linear_combination -key⟩\n251\t\n252\t/-- **`Fin n`-indexed diagonalization.** `QuadraticForm.equivalent_weightedSumSquares` produces weights\n253\tindexed by `Fin (finrank …)`; reindexing along `finrank (Fin n → ℚ) = n` gives a diagonalization indexed by\n254\t`Fin n` directly, removing the `Fin (finrank)` vs `Fin n` friction in the assembly. -/\n255\ttheorem equivalent_weightedSumSquares_fin {n : ℕ} (A : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℚ) :\n256\t    ∃ w : Fin n → ℚ, A.toQuadraticMap'.Equivalent (QuadraticMap.weightedSumSquares ℚ w) := by\n257\t  obtain ⟨w, hwe⟩ := QuadraticForm.equivalent_weightedSumSquares A.toQuadraticMap'\n258\t  set e : Fin (Module.finrank ℚ (Fin n → ℚ)) ≃ Fin n := finCongr (Module.finrank_fin_fun ℚ)\n259\t  refine ⟨w ∘ e.symm, hwe.trans ⟨⟨LinearEquiv.funCongrLeft ℚ ℚ e.symm, fun m => ?_⟩⟩⟩\n260\t  rw [QuadraticMap.weightedSumSquares_apply, QuadraticMap.weightedSumSquares_apply,\n261\t      ← Equiv.sum_comp e.symm (fun i => w i • (m i * m i))]\n262\t  refine Finset.sum_congr rfl fun x _ => ?_\n263\t  simp [LinearEquiv.funCongrLeft_apply, LinearMap.funLeft_apply, Function.comp]\n264\t\n265\t/-! ## Rank-4 even unimodular forms have determinant `+1`\n266\t\n267\tThe signature of a rank-4 even unimodular form is `0` (the `det = -1`, `σ = ±2` shapes do not occur): mod 4,\n268\t`det ≡ (A₀₁A₂₃ - A₀₂A₁₃ + A₀₃A₁₂)²` (the diagonal entries are even, so every determinant term containing a\n269\tdiagonal factor is `≡ 0 mod 4`), a square `∈ {0,1}`; with `det = ±1` this forces `det = 1`. This pins the\n270\tdiscriminant of the diagonalization to a **square**, so the square-discriminant machinery below applies. -/\n271\t\n272\tset_option maxRecDepth 4000 in\n273\t/-- **Mod-4 determinant of an even-diagonal symmetric `4×4`.** The determinant equals the squared\n274\t\"Pfaffian-like\" combination `(af - be + cd)²` modulo `4` (every term with a diagonal factor `2gᵢ` is `≡0`). -/\n275\ttheorem det4_evenDiag_sq (g0 g1 g2 g3 a b c d e f : ZMod 4) :\n276\t    (!![2 * g0, a, b, c; a, 2 * g1, d, e; b, d, 2 * g2, f; c, e, f, 2 * g3] :\n277\t        Matrix (Fin 4) (Fin 4) (ZMod 4)).det = (a * f - b * e + c * d) ^ 2 := by\n278\t  have h4 : (4 : ZMod 4) = 0 := by decide\n279\t  rw [Matrix.det_succ_row_zero]\n280\t  simp [Fin.sum_univ_four, Matrix.det_fin_three, Matrix.submatrix_apply, Fin.succAbove,\n281\t    Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one, Matrix.head_cons]\n282\t  linear_combination (g0 * g1 * g2 * g3 * 4 - g0 * g1 * f ^ 2 - g0 * g2 * e ^ 2 - g0 * g3 * d ^ 2\n283\t    + g0 * f * d * e - g1 * g2 * c ^ 2 - g1 * g3 * b ^ 2 + g1 * f * b * c - g2 * g3 * a ^ 2\n284\t    + g2 * e * a * c + g3 * d * a * b - f * d * a * c) * h4\n285\t\n286\t/-- **A rank-4 even unimodular form has `det = 1`.** (Mod-4: `det ≡ (A₀₁A₂₃ - A₀₂A₁₃ + A₀₃A₁₂)² ∈ {0,1}`;\n287\twith `det = ±1` this forces `det = 1`, excluding the `σ = ±2` shapes — so the diagonalization has square\n288\tdiscriminant.) -/\n289\ttheorem det_eq_one_of_evenUnimodular_four (A : Matrix (Fin 4) (Fin 4) ℤ) (hA : IsEvenUnimodular A) :\n290\t    A.det = 1 := by\n291\t  obtain ⟨hsym, hdet, heven⟩ := hA\n292\t  obtain ⟨g0, hg0⟩ := heven 0\n293\t  obtain ⟨g1, hg1⟩ := heven 1\n294\t  obtain ⟨g2, hg2⟩ := heven 2\n295\t  obtain ⟨g3, hg3⟩ := heven 3\n296\t  have s10 : A 1 0 = A 0 1 := by\n297\t    have h := congrFun (congrFun hsym 0) 1; simpa [Matrix.transpose_apply] using h\n298\t  have s20 : A 2 0 = A 0 2 := by\n299\t    have h := congrFun (congrFun hsym 0) 2; simpa [Matrix.transpose_apply] using h\n300\t  have s30 : A 3 0 = A 0 3 := by\n301\t    have h := congrFun (congrFun hsym 0) 3; simpa [Matrix.transpose_apply] using h\n302\t  have s21 : A 2 1 = A 1 2 := by\n303\t    have h := congrFun (congrFun hsym 1) 2; simpa [Matrix.transpose_apply] using h\n304\t  have s31 : A 3 1 = A 1 3 := by\n305\t    have h := congrFun (congrFun hsym 1) 3; simpa [Matrix.transpose_apply] using h\n306\t  have s32 : A 3 2 = A 2 3 := by\n307\t    have h := congrFun (congrFun hsym 2) 3; simpa [Matrix.transpose_apply] using h\n308\t  have hM : A.map (Int.castRingHom (ZMod 4)) =\n309\t      !![2 * (g0 : ZMod 4), (A 0 1 : ZMod 4), (A 0 2 : ZMod 4), (A 0 3 : ZMod 4);\n310\t         (A 0 1 : ZMod 4), 2 * (g1 : ZMod 4), (A 1 2 : ZMod 4), (A 1 3 : ZMod 4);\n311\t         (A 0 2 : ZMod 4), (A 1 2 : ZMod 4), 2 * (g2 : ZMod 4), (A 2 3 : ZMod 4);\n312\t         (A 0 3 : ZMod 4), (A 1 3 : ZMod 4), (A 2 3 : ZMod 4), 2 * (g3 : ZMod 4)] := by\n313\t    ext i j\n314\t    fin_cases i <;> fin_cases j <;>\n315\t      simp only [Matrix.map_apply, Int.coe_castRingHom, Matrix.cons_val', Matrix.cons_val_zero,\n316\t        Matrix.cons_val_one, Matrix.head_cons, Matrix.head_fin_const, Matrix.cons_val_fin_one,\n317\t        Matrix.empty_val', Fin.isValue, Matrix.of_apply] <;>\n318\t      push_cast [hg0, hg1, hg2, hg3, s10, s20, s30, s21, s31, s32] <;> ring\n319\t  have hmod : (A.det : ZMod 4)\n320\t      = ((A 0 1 * A 2 3 - A 0 2 * A 1 3 + A 0 3 * A 1 2 : ℤ) : ZMod 4) ^ 2 := by\n321\t    have hd : (A.map (Int.castRingHom (ZMod 4))).det = (A.det : ZMod 4) :=\n322\t      (RingHom.map_det (Int.castRingHom (ZMod 4)) A).symm\n323\t    rw [← hd, hM, det4_evenDiag_sq]\n324\t    push_cast; ring\n325\t  rcases hdet with h | h\n326\t  · exact h\n327\t  · exfalso\n328\t    rw [h] at hmod\n329\t    have hsq : ∀ x : ZMod 4, ((-1 : ℤ) : ZMod 4) ≠ x ^ 2 := by decide\n330\t    exact hsq _ hmod\n331\t\n332\t/-! ## p = 2 from reciprocity (the lone sub-frontier, square-discriminant case)\n333\t\n334\tFor a square-discriminant rank-4 form the local isotropy at a place `v` is governed by a *single* binary\n335\tHilbert symbol `(α, β)_v` (`α = -ab`, `β = ac`) via the norm-form reduction\n336\t`quaternary_sqdisc_iso_iff_ternary` + the per-place symbol bridges. The product formula\n337\t`hilbertGlobalProd_eq_one` then forces the `p = 2` factor once all other places are `1` — so a\n338\tsquare-disc form isotropic at `ℝ` and every *odd* prime is automatically isotropic at `2`. -/\n339\t\n340\topen HilbertSymbol in\n341\t/-- **Reciprocity completeness for the binary symbol.** If `(α,β)_∞ = 1` and `(α,β)_p = 1` for every odd\n342\tprime `p`, then `(α,β)_2 = 1` — the `p = 2` factor is pinned by the Hilbert product formula\n343\t`hilbertGlobalProd_eq_one` (every other factor being `1`). -/\n344\ttheorem hilbertPrime_two_eq_one_of_real_odd {α β : ℤ} (hα : α ≠ 0) (hβ : β ≠ 0)\n345\t    (hreal : HilbertSymbol.hilbertReal (α : ℝ) (β : ℝ) = 1)\n346\t    (hodd : ∀ p : ℕ, p.Prime → p ≠ 2 → HilbertSymbol.hilbertPrime p α β = 1) :\n347\t    HilbertSymbol.hilbertPrime 2 α β = 1 := by\n348\t  have hglob := hilbertGlobalProd_eq_one hα hβ\n349\t  rw [hilbertGlobalProd] at hglob\n350\t  have hfin : ∏ᶠ p : ℕ, HilbertSymbol.hilbertPrime p α β = HilbertSymbol.hilbertPrime 2 α β := by\n351\t    refine finprod_eq_single _ 2 (fun p hp2 => ?_)\n352\t    by_cases hpp : p.Prime\n353\t    · exact hodd p hpp hp2\n354\t    · exact hilbertPrime_of_not_prime hpp α β\n355\t  rw [hfin, hreal, one_mul] at hglob\n356\t  exact hglob\n357\t\n358\t/-- **Square-discriminant quaternary Hasse–Minkowski, without the place `2`.** For nonzero integers with\n359\t`a·b·c·d = s²`, if `a x² + b y² − c z² − d w² = 0` is solvable over `ℝ` and over every *odd* `ℚ_p`, it is\n360\tsolvable over `ℚ`. The `p = 2` local solvability — the lone gap in the shipped\n361\t`quaternary_sqdisc_solvable_of_local` — is supplied by reciprocity: each place's isotropy is the symbol\n362\t`(−ab, ac)_v = 1`, and `hilbertPrime_two_eq_one_of_real_odd` pins the `2`-factor from the others. -/\n363\ttheorem quaternary_sqdisc_solvable_of_local_no_two {a b c d s : ℤ}\n364\t    (ha : a ≠ 0) (hb : b ≠ 0) (hc : c ≠ 0) (hd : d ≠ 0) (hsq : a * b * c * d = s ^ 2)\n365\t    (hR : ∃ x y z w : ℝ, ¬(x = 0 ∧ y = 0 ∧ z = 0 ∧ w = 0) ∧\n366\t      (a : ℝ) * x ^ 2 + (b : ℝ) * y ^ 2 - (c : ℝ) * z ^ 2 - (d : ℝ) * w ^ 2 = 0)\n367\t    (hodd : ∀ (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime], p ≠ 2 → ∃ x y z w : ℚ_[p],\n368\t      ¬(x = 0 ∧ y = 0 ∧ z = 0 ∧ w = 0) ∧\n369\t      (a : ℚ_[p]) * x ^ 2 + (b : ℚ_[p]) * y ^ 2 - (c : ℚ_[p]) * z ^ 2 - (d : ℚ_[p]) * w ^ 2 = 0) :\n370\t    ∃ x y z w : ℚ, ¬(x = 0 ∧ y = 0 ∧ z = 0 ∧ w = 0) ∧\n371\t      (a : ℚ) * x ^ 2 + (b : ℚ) * y ^ 2 - (c : ℚ) * z ^ 2 - (d : ℚ) * w ^ 2 = 0 := by\n372\t  set α : ℤ := -(a * b) with hαdef\n373\t  set β : ℤ := a * c with hβdef\n374\t  have hα : α ≠ 0 := neg_ne_zero.mpr (mul_ne_zero ha hb)\n375\t  have hβ : β ≠ 0 := mul_ne_zero ha hc\n376\t  -- real place: the symbol (α,β)_∞ = 1\n377\t  have hreal : HilbertSymbol.hilbertReal (α : ℝ) (β : ℝ) = 1 := by\n378\t    obtain ⟨X, Y, Z, hnzt, het⟩ :=\n379\t      (quaternary_sqdisc_iso_iff_ternary (a := (a : ℝ)) (b := (b : ℝ)) (c := (c : ℝ)) (d := (d : ℝ))\n380\t        (s := (s : ℝ)) (by exact_mod_cast ha) (by exact_mod_cast hb) (by exact_mod_cast hc)\n381\t        (by exact_mod_cast hd) (by exact_mod_cast hsq)).mp hR\n382\t    have het' : Z ^ 2 = (α : ℝ) * X ^ 2 + (β : ℝ) * Y ^ 2 := by\n383\t      rw [het, hαdef, hβdef]; push_cast; ring\n384\t    rw [HilbertSymbol.hilbertReal_eq_one_iff (by exact_mod_cast hα) (by exact_mod_cast hβ)]\n385\t    refine ⟨X, Y, Z, ?_, het'⟩\n386\t    intro h\n387\t    rw [Prod.mk_eq_zero] at h\n388\t    obtain ⟨hX, hYZ⟩ := h\n389\t    rw [Prod.mk_eq_zero] at hYZ\n390\t    exact hnzt ⟨hX, hYZ.1, hYZ.2⟩\n391\t  -- odd primes: the symbol (α,β)_p = 1\n392\t  have hoddsym : ∀ p : ℕ, p.Prime → p ≠ 2 → HilbertSymbol.hilbertPrime p α β = 1 := by\n393\t    intro p hp hp2\n394\t    haveI := Fact.mk hp\n395\t    rw [HilbertSymbol.hilbertPrime_odd hp hp2, ← solvable_padic_iff_hilbertPadicInt_one hp2 hα hβ]\n396\t    obtain ⟨X, Y, Z, hnzt, het⟩ :=\n397\t      (quaternary_sqdisc_iso_iff_ternary (a := (a : ℚ_[p])) (b := (b : ℚ_[p])) (c := (c : ℚ_[p]))\n398\t        (d := (d : ℚ_[p])) (s := (s : ℚ_[p])) (by exact_mod_cast ha) (by exact_mod_cast hb)\n399\t        (by exact_mod_cast hc) (by exact_mod_cast hd) (by exact_mod_cast hsq)).mp (hodd p hp2)\n400\t    exact ⟨X, Y, Z, hnzt, by rw [het, hαdef, hβdef]; push_cast; ring⟩\n401\t  -- p = 2 from reciprocity\n402\t  have h2sym : HilbertSymbol.hilbert2Int α β = 1 := by\n403\t    rw [← HilbertSymbol.hilbertPrime_two]\n404\t    exact hilbertPrime_two_eq_one_of_real_odd hα hβ hreal hoddsym\n405\t  have h2loc : ∃ x y z w : ℚ_[2], ¬(x = 0 ∧ y = 0 ∧ z = 0 ∧ w = 0) ∧\n406\t      (a : ℚ_[2]) * x ^ 2 + (b : ℚ_[2]) * y ^ 2 - (c : ℚ_[2]) * z ^ 2 - (d : ℚ_[2]) * w ^ 2 = 0 := by\n407\t    haveI : Fact (Nat.Prime 2) := ⟨Nat.prime_two⟩\n408\t    obtain ⟨X, Y, Z, hnzt, het⟩ := (solvable_2adic_iff_hilbert2Int hα hβ).mpr h2sym\n409\t    refine (quaternary_sqdisc_iso_iff_ternary (a := (a : ℚ_[2])) (b := (b : ℚ_[2])) (c := (c : ℚ_[2]))\n410\t      (d := (d : ℚ_[2])) (s := (s : ℚ_[2])) (by exact_mod_cast ha) (by exact_mod_cast hb)\n411\t      (by exact_mod_cast hc) (by exact_mod_cast hd) (by exact_mod_cast hsq)).mpr ⟨X, Y, Z, hnzt, ?_⟩\n412\t    rw [het, hαdef, hβdef]; push_cast; ring\n413\t  -- combine all places and invoke the full square-disc Hasse–Minkowski\n414\t  have hloc : ∀ (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime], ∃ x y z w : ℚ_[p], ¬(x = 0 ∧ y = 0 ∧ z = 0 ∧ w = 0) ∧\n415\t      (a : ℚ_[p]) * x ^ 2 + (b : ℚ_[p]) * y ^ 2 - (c : ℚ_[p]) * z ^ 2 - (d : ℚ_[p]) * w ^ 2 = 0 := by\n416\t    intro p _\n417\t    by_cases hp2 : p = 2\n418\t    · subst hp2; exact h2loc\n419\t    · exact hodd p hp2\n420\t  have hQ := quaternary_sqdisc_solvable_of_local (a := (a : ℚ)) (b := (b : ℚ)) (c := (c : ℚ))\n421\t    (d := (d : ℚ)) (s := (s : ℚ)) (by exact_mod_cast ha) (by exact_mod_cast hb) (by exact_mod_cast hc)\n422\t    (by exact_mod_cast hd) (by exact_mod_cast hsq) ?_ ?_\n423\t  · obtain ⟨x, y, z, w, hnz, he⟩ := hQ\n424\t    exact ⟨x, y, z, w, hnz, by push_cast at he ⊢; linear_combination he⟩\n425\t  · obtain ⟨x, y, z, w, hnz, he⟩ := hR\n426\t    exact ⟨x, y, z, w, hnz, by push_cast at he ⊢; linear_combination he⟩\n427\t  · intro p _\n428\t    obtain ⟨x, y, z, w, hnz, he⟩ := hloc p\n429\t    exact ⟨x, y, z, w, hnz, by push_cast at he ⊢; linear_combination he⟩\n430\t\n431\t/-- **Square-discriminant rank-4 diagonal Hasse–Minkowski without place 2**, in `∑ dᵢ xᵢ²` form. A diagonal\n432\tform with nonzero integer coefficients and square product, isotropic over `ℝ` and every *odd* `ℚ_p`, is\n433\tisotropic over `ℚ`. A thin `Fin 4`-vector wrapper over `quaternary_sqdisc_solvable_of_local_no_two`. -/\n434\ttheorem diag_four_solvable_of_local_no_two {d : Fin 4 → ℤ} (hne : ∀ i, d i ≠ 0)\n435\t    (hsq : IsSquare (d 0 * d 1 * d 2 * d 3))\n436\t    (hR : ∃ x : Fin 4 → ℝ, x ≠ 0 ∧ ∑ i, (d i : ℝ) * x i ^ 2 = 0)\n437\t    (hodd : ∀ (p : ℕ) [Fact p.Prime], p ≠ 2 →\n438\t      ∃ x : Fin 4 → ℚ_[p], x ≠ 0 ∧ ∑ i, (d i : ℚ_[p]) * x i ^ 2 = 0) :\n439\t    ∃ x : Fin 4 → ℚ, x ≠ 0 ∧ ∑ i, (d i : ℚ) * x i ^ 2 = 0 := by\n440\t  obtain ⟨s, hs⟩ := hsq\n441\t  obtain ⟨x, y, z, w, hnz, he⟩ := quaternary_sqdisc_solvable_of_local_no_two\n442\t    (hne 0) (hne 1) (neg_ne_zero.mpr (hne 2)) (neg_ne_zero.mpr (hne 3))\n443\t    (show d 0 * d 1 * (-d 2) * (-d 3) = s ^ 2 by rw [pow_two]; linear_combination hs)\n444\t    (by obtain ⟨v, hv, hve⟩ := hR\n445\t        refine ⟨v 0, v 1, v 2, v 3, fun h => hv (funext fun i => by fin_cases i <;> simp_all), ?_⟩\n446\t        rw [Fin.sum_univ_four] at hve; push_cast; linear_combination hve)\n447\t    (by intro p _ hp2\n448\t        obtain ⟨v, hv, hve⟩ := hodd p hp2\n449\t        refine ⟨v 0, v 1, v 2, v 3, fun h => hv (funext fun i => by fin_cases i <;> simp_all), ?_⟩\n450\t        rw [Fin.sum_univ_four] at hve; push_cast; linear_combination hve)\n451\t  refine ⟨![x, y, z, w], fun h => hnz ⟨?_, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩, ?_⟩\n452\t  · have := congrFun h 0; simpa using this\n453\t  · have := congrFun h 1; simpa using this\n454\t  · have := congrFun h 2; simpa using this\n455\t  · have := congrFun h 3; simpa using this\n456\t  · rw [Fin.sum_univ_four]; simp only [Matrix.cons_val_zero, Matrix.cons_val_one, Matrix.head_cons,\n457\t      Matrix.cons_val_two, Matrix.cons_val_three, Matrix.tail_cons]\n458\t    push_cast at he ⊢; linear_combination he\n459\t\n460\t/-- A ring hom commutes with the Gram quadratic value `v ⬝ᵥ M *ᵥ v`. -/\n461\ttheorem ringHom_map_gram {R S : Type*} [CommRing R] [CommRing S] (ι : R →+* S) {k : ℕ}\n462\t    (M : Matrix (Fin k) (Fin k) R) (v : Fin k → R) :\n463\t    ι (v ⬝ᵥ M *ᵥ v) = (fun i => ι (v i)) ⬝ᵥ (M.map ι) *ᵥ (fun i => ι (v i)) := by\n464\t  simp only [dotProduct, Matrix.mulVec, map_sum, map_mul, Matrix.map_apply]\n465\t\n466\t/-- **Weak [HM] at rank 4.** Every indefinite even unimodular `4×4` form has a nonzero integer isotropic\n467\tvector. `det = 1` (excluding `σ = ±2`) makes the ℚ-diagonalization square-discriminant; ℝ-isotropy is\n468\tindefiniteness; odd-`p` isotropy is the `ℤ_p`-unimodular fact (brick a) transported through the explicit\n469\tcongruence; the `p = 2` place is then free by reciprocity (brick b). -/\n470\ttheorem weakIsotropic_rank_four (A : Matrix (Fin 4) (Fin 4) ℤ) (hA : IsEvenUnimodular A)\n471\t    (hsp : 0 < sigPos (A.map (Int.cast : ℤ → ℝ)).toQuadraticMap')\n472\t    (hsn : 0 < sigNeg (A.map (Int.cast : ℤ → ℝ)).toQuadraticMap') :\n473\t    ∃ v : Fin 4 → ℤ, v ≠ 0 ∧ v ⬝ᵥ A *ᵥ v = 0 := by\n474\t  apply exists_int_isotropic_of_rat A\n475\t  set Aq : Matrix (Fin 4) (Fin 4) ℚ := A.map (Int.cast : ℤ → ℚ) with hAq\n476\t  have hAqsymm : Aq.IsSymm := by\n477\t    ext i j\n478\t    rw [Matrix.transpose_apply, hAq, Matrix.map_apply, Matrix.map_apply]\n479\t    congr 1\n480\t    have := congrFun (congrFun hA.1 i) j; rwa [Matrix.transpose_apply] at this\n481\t  have hdetq : Aq.det = 1 := by\n482\t    have h : Aq.det = ((A.det : ℤ) : ℚ) := (RingHom.map_det (Int.castRingHom ℚ) A).symm\n483\t    rw [h, det_eq_one_of_evenUnimodular_four A hA, Int.cast_one]\n484\t  obtain ⟨w, hwe⟩ := equivalent_weightedSumSquares_fin Aq\n485\t  obtain ⟨P, hPunit, hAeq⟩ := congr_of_equiv_weighted Aq hAqsymm hwe\n486\t  have hsqw : IsSquare (∏ i, w i) := isSquare_prod_weights Aq hAqsymm hdetq hwe\n487\t  -- real signs of w (from indefiniteness)\n488\t  have hspq : 0 < sigPos Aq.toQuadraticMap' := sigPos_cast_pos A hsp\n489\t  have hsnq : 0 < sigNeg Aq.toQuadraticMap' := sigNeg_cast_pos A hsn\n490\t  rw [sigPos_of_equiv_weightedSumSquares hwe] at hspq\n491\t  rw [sigNeg_of_equiv_weightedSumSquares hwe] at hsnq\n492\t  obtain ⟨ip, hip⟩ : ∃ i, 0 < w i := Set.nonempty_of_ncard_ne_zero hspq.ne'\n493\t  obtain ⟨iN, hiN⟩ : ∃ i, w i < 0 := Set.nonempty_of_ncard_ne_zero hsnq.ne'\n494\t  rw [matrix_isotropic_iff_weighted Aq w hwe]\n495\t  by_cases hz : ∃ i, w i = 0\n496\t  · obtain ⟨i0, hi0⟩ := hz\n497\t    exact ⟨fun j => if j = i0 then 1 else 0, fun h => by simpa using congrFun h i0, by\n498\t      rw [Finset.sum_eq_single i0]\n499\t      · simp [hi0]\n500\t      · intro b _ hb; simp [hb]\n501\t      · intro h; simp at h⟩\n502\t  · simp only [not_exists] at hz\n503\t    set d : Fin 4 → ℤ := fun i => (w i).num * ((w i).den : ℤ) with hd\n504\t    have hdne : ∀ i, d i ≠ 0 := fun i =>\n505\t      mul_ne_zero (Rat.num_ne_zero.mpr (hz i)) (by exact_mod_cast (w i).den_nz)\n506\t    have hiff := diag_iso_rat_int (K := ℚ) w\n507\t    simp only [Rat.cast_id] at hiff\n508\t    rw [hiff]\n509\t    -- IsSquare (∏ d)\n510\t    have hsqd : IsSquare (d 0 * d 1 * d 2 * d 3) := by\n511\t      rw [← Rat.isSquare_intCast_iff]\n512\t      have hnum : ∀ i, ((w i).num : ℚ) = (w i) * ((w i).den : ℚ) :=\n513\t        fun i => (div_eq_iff (by exact_mod_cast (w i).den_nz)).mp (Rat.num_div_den (w i))\n514\t      have hcast : ((d 0 * d 1 * d 2 * d 3 : ℤ) : ℚ)\n515\t          = (∏ i, w i) * (((w 0).den * (w 1).den * (w 2).den * (w 3).den : ℕ) : ℚ) ^ 2 := by\n516\t        simp only [hd, Fin.prod_univ_four]\n517\t        push_cast\n518\t        rw [hnum 0, hnum 1, hnum 2, hnum 3]; ring\n519\t      rw [hcast]\n520\t      exact hsqw.mul ⟨_, pow_two _⟩\n521\t    refine diag_four_solvable_of_local_no_two hdne hsqd ?_ ?_\n522\t    · -- ℝ: indefinite ⟹ a + and − coefficient among (d i : ℝ)\n523\t      have hdR_pos : 0 < ((d ip : ℤ) : ℝ) := by\n524\t        rw [hd]; have : (0:ℤ) < (w ip).num * (w ip).den :=\n525\t          mul_pos (Rat.num_pos.mpr hip) (by exact_mod_cast (w ip).pos)\n526\t        exact_mod_cast this\n527\t      have hdR_neg : ((d iN : ℤ) : ℝ) < 0 := by\n528\t        rw [hd]; have : (w iN).num * ((w iN).den : ℤ) < 0 :=\n529\t          mul_neg_of_neg_of_pos (Rat.num_neg.mpr hiN) (by exact_mod_cast (w iN).pos)\n530\t        exact_mod_cast this\n531\t      have hipN : ip ≠ iN := fun h => by rw [h] at hip; exact absurd (hip.trans hiN) (lt_irrefl _)\n532\t      exact diag_real_isotropic_of_signs (fun i => (d i : ℝ)) ip iN hipN hdR_pos hdR_neg\n533\t    · -- odd p: brick (a) + congruence transfer\n534\t      intro p _ hp2\n535\t      set φ : ℚ →+* ℚ_[p] := Rat.castHom ℚ_[p] with hφ\n536\t      -- A over ℤ_p isotropic (brick a)\n537\t      have hsymZp : (A.map (Int.castRingHom ℤ_[p])).transpose = A.map (Int.castRingHom ℤ_[p]) := by\n538\t        ext i j\n539\t        simp only [Matrix.transpose_apply, Matrix.map_apply]\n540\t        congr 1\n541\t        have := congrFun (congrFun hA.1 i) j; rwa [Matrix.transpose_apply] at this\n542\t      have hunitZp : IsUnit ((A.map (Int.castRingHom ℤ_[p])).det) := by\n543\t        have hdt : (A.map (Int.castRingHom ℤ_[p])).det = (Int.castRingHom ℤ_[p]) A.det :=\n544\t          (RingHom.map_det (Int.castRingHom ℤ_[p]) A).symm\n545\t        rw [hdt]\n546\t        rcases hA.2.1 with h | h <;> rw [h] <;> simp\n547\t      obtain ⟨vp, hvp0, hvpe⟩ :=\n548\t        isotropic_padicInt_of_unit_det hp2 (by norm_num) _ hsymZp hunitZp\n549\t      -- cast ℤ_p → ℚ_p : A.map(ℤ→ℚ_p) isotropic\n550\t      set ι : ℤ_[p] →+* ℚ_[p] := algebraMap ℤ_[p] ℚ_[p] with hι\n551\t      have hAQp_iso : ∃ x : Fin 4 → ℚ_[p], x ≠ 0 ∧\n552\t          x ⬝ᵥ (A.map (Int.cast : ℤ → ℚ_[p])) *ᵥ x = 0 := by\n553\t        have hinj : Function.Injective ι := FaithfulSMul.algebraMap_injective ℤ_[p] ℚ_[p]\n554\t        refine ⟨fun i => ι (vp i), fun h => hvp0 (funext fun i => ?_), ?_⟩\n555\t        · have hc := congrFun h i\n556\t          exact hinj ((show ι (vp i) = 0 by simpa using hc).trans (map_zero ι).symm)\n557\t        · have hmateq : A.map (Int.cast : ℤ → ℚ_[p])\n558\t              = (A.map (Int.castRingHom ℤ_[p])).map ι := by\n559\t            ext i j; simp [Matrix.map_apply, hι, map_intCast]\n560\t          have hg := ringHom_map_gram ι (A.map (Int.castRingHom ℤ_[p])) vp\n561\t          rw [hvpe, map_zero] at hg\n562\t          rw [hmateq]; exact hg.symm\n563\t      -- transfer: A_{ℚ_p} = Aq.map φ = (P.map φ)ᵀ * diagonal (φ∘w) * (P.map φ)\n564\t      have hAcast : A.map (Int.cast : ℤ → ℚ_[p]) = Aq.map φ := by\n565\t        ext i j; simp only [hAq, Matrix.map_apply, hφ, map_intCast]\n566\t      have hAeqp : Aq.map φ = (P.map φ)ᵀ * Matrix.diagonal (fun i => φ (w i)) * (P.map φ) := by\n567\t        rw [hAeq]\n568\t        rw [Matrix.map_mul, Matrix.map_mul, Matrix.transpose_map, Matrix.diagonal_map (map_zero φ)]\n569\t      have hPunitp : IsUnit (P.map φ).det := by\n570\t        have hdt : (P.map φ).det = φ P.det := (RingHom.map_det φ P).symm\n571\t        rw [hdt]; exact hPunit.map φ\n572\t      have hdiagiso : ∃ x : Fin 4 → ℚ_[p], x ≠ 0 ∧\n573\t          x ⬝ᵥ Matrix.diagonal (fun i => φ (w i)) *ᵥ x = 0 := by\n574\t        rw [matrix_isotropic_congr (Matrix.diagonal (fun i => φ (w i))) (P.map φ) hPunitp]\n575\t        rw [← hAeqp, ← hAcast]; exact hAQp_iso\n576\t      obtain ⟨x, hx0, hxe⟩ := hdiagiso\n577\t      have hsum : ∑ i, (w i : ℚ_[p]) * x i ^ 2 = 0 := by\n578\t        rw [← hxe, dotProduct]; refine Finset.sum_congr rfl fun i _ => ?_\n579\t        rw [Matrix.mulVec_diagonal]; simp only [hφ, Rat.coe_castHom]; ring\n580\t      exact (diag_iso_rat_int (K := ℚ_[p]) w).mp ⟨x, hx0, hsum⟩\n581\t\n582\t/-! ## Discharge of `HasWeakIsotropicVectorHyp` (all ranks) -/\n583\t\n584\t/-- **The weak [HM] hypothesis is a theorem.** Every indefinite even unimodular integer form has a nonzero\n585\tinteger isotropic vector. Rank case-split: rank 0 (`sigPos = 0`, contradiction), ranks 1 & 3 (no even\n586\tunimodular form exists), rank 2 (`weakIsotropic_rank_two`), rank 4 (`weakIsotropic_rank_four`), rank ≥ 5\n587\t(`weakIsotropic_of_five_le`). -/\n588\ttheorem hasWeakIsotropicVector : HasWeakIsotropicVectorHyp := by\n589\t  intro m A hA hsp hsn\n590\t  rcases Nat.lt_or_ge m 5 with hm | hm\n591\t  · interval_cases m\n592\t    · exfalso\n593\t      have h := QuadraticForm.sigPos_add_sigNeg_add_radical\n594\t        (Q := (A.map (Int.cast : ℤ → ℝ)).toQuadraticMap')\n595\t      rw [show : ℕ) (M : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) ℤ) (heu : IsEvenUnimodular M) :\n609\t    8 ∣ latticeSig M :=\n610\t  eight_dvd_latticeSig_of_HM hasIsotropicVector n M heu\n611\t\n612\t/-- **`16 ∣ σ` for every even
