{"parentUuid":"a82e839f-5910-43d1-991f-eccb4044b529","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a5ea667e8aa34be02","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016QihnSNrRE2asbD9jgP1kf","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/-\n2\tPhase 5q.B: the signature of E₈ is `+8` (and of `−E₈` is `−8`), kernel-pure.\n3\t\n4\tThis is sub-step [A1] of the FULL (zero-axiom, zero-citation) discharge of van der Blij's\n5\t`IsEvenUnimodular M → 8 ∣ latticeSig M` via the classification of even unimodular lattices\n6\t(`E₈^a ⊕ (−E₈)^b ⊕ H^c`). The generators' signatures are the numerical anchors of that classification:\n7\t`σ(E₈) = 8`, `σ(−E₈) = −8`, `σ(H) = 0`. This module nails the first two.\n8\t\n9\tTHE OBSTRUCTION AND HOW WE ROUTE AROUND IT. The signature is *analytic* (a count of eigenvalue signs),\n10\tso proving `σ(E₈) = 8` means proving `E₈` is positive definite over ℝ. The textbook certificate is an\n11\tLDLᵀ decomposition with positive pivots, but its pivots are rational (`D = diag(2,2,3/2,5/6,4/5,3/4,\n12\t2/3,1/2)`), and kernel `decide` does **not** reduce ℚ matrix identities (Rat normalisation gets stuck),\n13\twhile an entrywise `norm_num` over the 64 entries blows the heartbeat budget.\n14\t\n15\tThe trick: `2·E₈` embeds in `ℤ⁸` (the E₈ root lattice has a half-integer basis, so twice the basis is\n16\tintegral). Concretely there is an **integer** matrix `C8` — its columns are `2 ×` the E₈ simple roots,\n17\tordered to match `E8lit` — with `C8ᵀ · C8 = 4 · E8lit`. That identity is a product of small integers, so\n18\t`decide` reduces it cleanly over ℤ. Casting to ℝ, `C8rᴴ · C8r = 4 · E8r`, and since `C8` is invertible\n19\t(`det(C8)² = det(C8ᵀ·C8) = det(4·E8lit) = 4⁸ · (±1) ≠ 0`, dodging the infeasible 8×8 determinant),\n20\t`Matrix.PosDef.conjTranspose_mul_self` gives `(4·E8r).PosDef`; stripping the positive scalar `4` gives\n21\t`E8r.PosDef`. Then `latticeSig_of_posDef` reads off `σ(E₈) = 8`.\n22\t\n23\tAll proofs are kernel-pure (`propext`/`Classical.choice`/`Quot.sound` only); no `native_decide`, no\n24\t`maxHeartbeats`, no axiom. Builds on `E8Literal.lean` (kernel-pure even-unimodular E₈) and\n25\t`LatticeSignature.lean` (`latticeSig`, `latticeSig_of_posDef`).\n26\t-/\n27\t\n28\timport Mathlib\n29\timport SKEFTHawking.E8Literal\n30\timport SKEFTHawking.LatticeSignature\n31\t\n32\tnamespace SKEFTHawking\n33\t\n34\topen Matrix\n35\t\n36\t/-- The integer matrix whose columns are `2 ×` the E₈ simple roots (Bourbaki even-coordinate model,\n37\tordered to match `E8lit`). Its defining property is `C8ᵀ · C8 = 4 · E8lit` (`c8_eq`). -/\n38\tdef C8 : Matrix (Fin 8) (Fin 8) ℤ :=\n39\t  !![1, 2, -2, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0;\n40\t     -1, 2, 2, -2, 0, 0, 0, 0;\n41\t     -1, 0, 0, 2, -2, 0, 0, 0;\n42\t     -1, 0, 0, 0, 2, -2, 0, 0;\n43\t     -1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 2, -2, 0;\n44\t     -1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 2, -2;\n45\t     -1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 2;\n46\t     1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0]\n47\t\n48\t/-- **The integral Gram identity** `C8ᵀ · C8 = 4 · E8lit`, proven by `decide` (small integer\n49\tmultiplications — kernel-pure, native_decide-free). This is the certificate that `4 · E8lit` is the Gram\n50\tmatrix of the integer vectors `2 × (E₈ roots)`, hence positive (semi)definite. -/\n51\ttheorem c8_eq : C8ᵀ * C8 = (4 : ℤ) • E8lit := by decide\n52\t\n53\t/-- `E8lit` cast to a real matrix. -/\n54\tnoncomputable def E8r : Matrix (Fin 8) (Fin 8) ℝ := E8lit.map (Int.cast : ℤ → ℝ)\n55\t\n56\t/-- `C8` cast to a real matrix. -/\n57\tnoncomputable def C8r : Matrix (Fin 8) (Fin 8) ℝ := C8.map (Int.cast : ℤ → ℝ)\n58\t\n59\t/-- The real Gram identity `C8rᴴ · C8r = 4 · E8r`, transported from the integral `c8_eq` along the\n60\tcast ring homomorphism `ℤ → ℝ` (over ℝ the conjugate transpose is the transpose). -/\n61\ttheorem c8r_eq : C8rᴴ * C8r = (4 : ℝ) • E8r := by\n62\t  have key := congrArg (fun M : Matrix (Fin 8) (Fin 8) ℤ => M.map (Int.castRingHom ℝ)) c8_eq\n63\t  rw [Matrix.map_mul, Matrix.transpose_map] at key\n64\t  simp only [Int.coe_castRingHom] at key\n65\t  rw [Matrix.conjTranspose_eq_transpose_of_trivial]\n66\t  unfold C8r E8r\n67\t  rw [key]\n68\t  ext i j\n69\t  simp only [Matrix.map_apply, Matrix.smul_apply, smul_eq_mul]\n70\t  push_cast\n71\t  ring\n72\t\n73\t/-- **E₈ is positive definite over ℝ.** From `C8rᴴ · C8r = 4 · E8r` with `C8r` invertible\n74\t(`det(C8r)² = det(4·E8r) = 4⁸ · det(E8r)`, and `det(E8r) = ±1 ≠ 0` by unimodularity — no 8×8 determinant\n75\tis ever evaluated), `Matrix.PosDef.conjTranspose_mul_self` gives `(4·E8r).PosDef`, and dividing by the\n76\tpositive scalar `4` gives `E8r.PosDef`. -/\n77\ttheorem e8r_posDef : E8r.PosDef := by\n78\t  have hE8det : E8r.det ≠ 0 := by\n79\t    have hd : E8r.det = ((E8lit.det : ℤ) : ℝ) := by rw [E8r, ← Int.cast_det]\n80\t    rcases e8lit_unimodular with h | h <;> rw [hd, h] <;> norm_num\n81\t  have hdet : IsUnit C8r.det := by\n82\t    rw [isUnit_iff_ne_zero]\n83\t    intro hzero\n84\t    have h2 : (C8rᴴ * C8r).det = 0 := by\n85\t      rw [Matrix.det_mul, Matrix.det_conjTranspose, hzero]; ring\n86\t    rw [c8r_eq, Matrix.det_smul] at h2\n87\t    simp only [Fintype.card_fin] at h2\n88\t    rcases mul_eq_zero.mp h2 with h | h\n89\t    · norm_num at h\n90\t    · exact hE8det h\n91\t  have hUnit : IsUnit C8r := (Matrix.isUnit_iff_isUnit_det C8r).mpr hdet\n92\t  have hpd : ((4 : ℝ) • E8r).PosDef := by\n93\t    rw [← c8r_eq]\n94\t    exact Matrix.PosDef.conjTranspose_mul_self C8r (Matrix.mulVec_injective_of_isUnit hUnit)\n95\t  have hfin := hpd.smul (a := (1 / 4 : ℝ)) (by norm_num)\n96\t  rwa [smul_smul, (by norm_num : (1 / 4 : ℝ) * 4 = 1), one_smul] at hfin\n97\t\n98\t/-- **`σ(E₈) = 8`** — the positive inertia index of E₈ is its full rank, since E₈ is positive definite. -/\n99\ttheorem e8lit_latticeSig : latticeSig E8lit = 8 := by\n100\t  have h := latticeSig_of_posDef E8lit e8r_posDef\n101\t  simpa using h\n102\t\n103\t/-- **`σ(−E₈) = −8`** — orientation reversal of `σ(E₈) = 8`. -/\n104\ttheorem neg_e8lit_latticeSig : latticeSig (-E8lit) = -8 := by\n105\t  rw [latticeSig_neg, e8lit_latticeSig]\n106\t\n107\tend SKEFTHawking\n108\t"}]},"uuid":"166edab8-a278-4ded-aa7a-d11fa69c9266","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:42:02.871Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"a82e839f-5910-43d1-991f-eccb4044b529","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"e94e2979-e203-4278-8334-46435bd75e88","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a5ea667e8aa34be02","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01SfGbnw3X3pW3tMjr47Cbup","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t/-\n2\tPhase 5q.B: kernel-pure even-unimodular E₈ (native_decide-free), via inverse exhibition.\n3\t\n4\tMathlib's `CartanMatrix.E₈` is defined *recursively* (built from E₆/E₇ via nested lambdas), so\n5\tkernel evaluation of its entries — and hence any kernel-pure `det`/`decide` proof about it — deep-\n6\trecurses and blows up. That is why `E8Lattice.lean` proves E₈'s det/even/symmetric facts with\n7\t`native_decide` (which the project is eliminating).\n8\t\n9\tThis module sidesteps the recursion by working with an explicit *literal* matrix `E8lit` (the E₈\n10\tCartan matrix written out; its entries were extracted from `CartanMatrix.E₈` and match it), and proves\n11\teven-unimodularity **kernel-pure** with a cheap trick: rather than evaluate the 8×8 determinant (the\n12\t40320-term Leibniz sum is infeasible in the kernel), we exhibit the explicit integer inverse `E8inv`\n13\t(the adjugate, an integer matrix because `det = 1`) and check `E8lit * E8inv = 1` by `decide` — only\n14\t512 small integer multiplications. `E8lit * E8inv = 1` gives `IsUnit (det E8lit)`, hence `det = ±1`\n15\t(unimodular); evenness and symmetry are direct `decide` on the literal.\n16\t\n17\tThis is a reusable, native_decide-free even-unimodular rank-8 lattice — the hardest concrete\n18\tprerequisite for the classification route to van der Blij's `8 ∣ σ` (`E₈^a ⊕ (-E₈)^b ⊕ H^c`), and a\n19\tcredible step in the discharge plan for the leg. All proofs are kernel-pure\n20\t(`propext`/`Classical.choice`/`Quot.sound` only); no `native_decide`, no `maxHeartbeats`, no axiom.\n21\t-/\n22\t\n23\timport Mathlib\n24\timport SKEFTHawking.AlgebraicRokhlin\n25\t\n26\tnamespace SKEFTHawking\n27\t\n28\topen Matrix\n29\t\n30\t/-- The E₈ Cartan matrix as an explicit literal `Matrix (Fin 8) (Fin 8) ℤ` (entries extracted from\n31\tand matching `CartanMatrix.E₈`). Working with the literal avoids the deep recursion of Mathlib's\n32\trecursive E₈ definition, keeping kernel `decide` cheap. -/\n33\tdef E8lit : Matrix (Fin 8) (Fin 8) ℤ :=\n34\t  !![2, 0, -1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0;\n35\t     0, 2, 0, -1, 0, 0, 0, 0;\n36\t     -1, 0, 2, -1, 0, 0, 0, 0;\n37\t     0, -1, -1, 2, -1, 0, 0, 0;\n38\t     0, 0, 0, -1, 2, -1, 0, 0;\n39\t     0, 0, 0, 0, -1, 2, -1, 0;\n40\t     0, 0, 0, 0, 0, -1, 2, -1;\n41\t     0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, -1, 2]\n42\t\n43\t/-- The explicit integer inverse of `E8lit` (its adjugate; integer because `det E8lit = 1`). -/\n44\tdef E8inv : Matrix (Fin 8) (Fin 8) ℤ :=\n45\t  !![4, 5, 7, 10, 8, 6, 4, 2;\n46\t     5, 8, 10, 15, 12, 9, 6, 3;\n47\t     7, 10, 14, 20, 16, 12, 8, 4;\n48\t     10, 15, 20, 30, 24, 18, 12, 6;\n49\t     8, 12, 16, 24, 20, 15, 10, 5;\n50\t     6, 9, 12, 18, 15, 12, 8, 4;\n51\t     4, 6, 8, 12, 10, 8, 6, 3;\n52\t     2, 3, 4, 6, 5, 4, 3, 2]\n53\t\n54\t/-- **The inverse-exhibition identity** `E8lit · E8inv = 1` — proven by `decide` (512 small integer\n55\tmultiplications), the cheap kernel-pure replacement for the 8×8 determinant computation. -/\n56\ttheorem e8lit_mul_inv : E8lit * E8inv = 1 := by decide\n57\t\n58\t/-- E₈ is symmetric (kernel-pure `decide` on the literal). -/\n59\ttheorem e8lit_symm : E8lit.transpose = E8lit := by decide\n60\t\n61\t/-- E₈ has even diagonal (kernel-pure `decide` on the literal). -/\n62\ttheorem e8lit_even : ∀ i, 2 ∣ E8lit i i := by decide\n63\t\n64\t/-- **E₈ is unimodular** (`det = ±1`), kernel-pure: from `E8lit · E8inv = 1` the determinant is a\n65\tunit in `ℤ`, hence `±1`. No determinant evaluation needed. -/\n66\ttheorem e8lit_unimodular : IsUnimodular E8lit := by\n67\t  have hdet : E8lit.det * E8inv.det = 1 := by\n68\t    rw [← Matrix.det_mul, e8lit_mul_inv, Matrix.det_one]\n69\t  rcases Int.eq_one_or_neg_one_of_mul_eq_one' hdet with ⟨h1, _⟩ | ⟨h1, _⟩\n70\t  · exact Or.inl h1\n71\t  · exact Or.inr h1\n72\t\n73\t/-- **E₈ is even unimodular**, kernel-pure (native_decide-free). The reusable even-unimodular rank-8\n74\tlattice for the classification route to van der Blij's `8 ∣ σ`. -/\n75\ttheorem e8lit_even_unimodular : IsEvenUnimodular E8lit :=\n76\t  ⟨e8lit_symm, e8lit_unimodular, e8lit_even⟩\n77\t\n78\tend SKEFTHawking\n79\t"}]},"uuid":"5de75a16-3329-427c-b1ef-5fac8252c8cd","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:42:09.340Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e94e2979-e203-4278-8334-46435bd75e88","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"040dd983-fa73-4649-9ea4-eaf7d377ffcb","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"d7b89794-41f0-46fe-9a20-6d198f8f624f","agentId":"a5ea667e8aa34be02","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Jk92wda7pBhtzs8MyFX4yg","type":"tool_result","content":"30\tnamespace SKEFTHawking\n31\t\n32\t/-! ## 1. Definitions for unimodular forms -/\n33\t\n34\t/-- A symmetric integer matrix is UNIMODULAR if its determinant is ±1. -/\n35\tdef IsUnimodular {n : Nat} (M : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) Int) : Prop :=\n36\t  M.det = 1 ∨ M.det = -1\n37\t\n38\t/-- A symmetric integer matrix is EVEN if all diagonal entries are even. -/\n39\tdef IsEven {n : Nat} (M : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) Int) : Prop :=\n40\t  ∀ i : Fin n, 2 ∣ M i i\n41\t\n42\t/-- A symmetric integer matrix is SYMMETRIC. -/\n43\tdef IsSymmetricInt {n : Nat} (M : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) Int) : Prop :=\n44\t  M.transpose = M\n45\t\n46\t/-- A symmetric integer matrix is EVEN UNIMODULAR if all three conditions hold. -/\n47\tdef IsEvenUnimodular {n : Nat} (M : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) Int) : Prop :=\n48\t  IsSymmetricInt M ∧ IsUnimodular M ∧ IsEven M\n49\t\n50\t/-! ## 2. Characteristic vectors -/\n51\t\n52\t/-- A characteristic vector c for a unimodular form M satisfies:\n53\t    for all x, (M * c) dot x ≡ x^T M x (mod 2).\n54\t    Equivalently: sum_j M_{ij} c_j ≡ M_{ii} (mod 2) for all i.\n55\t    This is the mod-2 reduction of the diagonal. -/\n56\tdef IsCharacteristic {n : Nat} (M : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) Int)\n57\t    (c : Fin n → Int) : Prop :=\n58\t  ∀ i : Fin n, (∑ j : Fin n, M i j * c j) % 2 = M i i % 2\n59\t\n60\t/--\n61\t**For an even form, the zero vector is characteristic.**\n62\t\n63\tProof: b(x,x) = sum_i M_{ii} x_i^2 + 2 sum_{i<j} M_{ij} x_i x_j.\n64\tIf all M_{ii} are even, then b(x,x) ≡ 0 mod 2 for all x.\n65\tAnd b(0,x) = 0 ≡ 0 mod 2. So 0 is characteristic.\n66\t\n67\tPROVIDED SOLUTION\n68\tIsCharacteristic M 0 unfolds to: for all i, (sum_j M_{ij} * 0) % 2 = M_{ii} % 2.\n69\tThe LHS is sum_j 0 = 0, so 0 % 2 = 0.\n70\tThe RHS is M_{ii} % 2, which equals 0 because IsEven gives 2 | M_{ii}.\n71\tSo both sides are 0.\n72\t-/\n73\ttheorem zero_is_characteristic_of_even {n : Nat}\n74\t    (M : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) Int) (heven : IsEven M) :\n75\t    IsCharacteristic M (fun _ => 0) := by\n76\t  intro i\n77\t  simp [IsCharacteristic, mul_zero, Finset.sum_const_zero]\n78\t  obtain ⟨k, hk⟩ := heven i\n79\t  rw [hk]; omega\n80\t\n81\t/-! ## 3. The key identity: c² ≡ σ mod 8\n82\t\n83\tThis is the deep algebraic result. For any unimodular form M with\n84\tcharacteristic vector c, the self-pairing c^T M c ≡ σ(M) mod 8.\n85\t\n86\tThe proof uses the classification of odd indefinite unimodular forms:\n87\tany such form is isomorphic to p<1> ⊕ q<-1> (diagonal ±1).\n88\tFor diagonal forms, (1,...,1,-1,...,-1) is characteristic with\n89\tc² = p - q = σ. And any two characteristic vectors satisfy c² ≡ c'² mod 8.\n90\t\n91\tWe enter this as a hypothesis (it requires the Hasse-Minkowski theorem\n92\tfor the classification, which is not in Mathlib).\n93\t-/\n94\t\n95\t/-- Self-pairing of a vector: c^T M c = sum_{i,j} c_i M_{ij} c_j. -/\n96\tdef selfPairing {n : Nat} (M : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) Int)\n97\t    (c : Fin n → Int) : Int :=\n98\t  ∑ i : Fin n, ∑ j : Fin n, c i * M i j * c j\n99\t\n100\t/--\n101\t**The key identity (hypothesis): for a unimodular form, any characteristic\n102\tvector c satisfies c^T M c ≡ σ(M) mod 8.**\n103\t\n104\tThis is Serre's theorem. The proof requires the classification of\n105\tindefinite unimodular forms (Hasse-Minkowski), which is not in Mathlib.\n106\tWe enter it as a hypothesis, clearly documented in HYPOTHESIS_REGISTRY.\n107\t-/\n108\tdef CharacteristicSquareModEight {n : Nat}\n109\t    (M : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) Int) (sigma : Int) : Prop :=\n110\t  ∀ c : Fin n → Int, IsCharacteristic M c →\n111\t    selfPairing M c % 8 = sigma % 8\n112\t\n113\t/-! ## 4. The Serre theorem: even unimodular → 8 | σ -/\n114\t\n115\t/--\n116\t**Algebraic Serre theorem: for an even unimodular form, σ ≡ 0 mod 8.**\n117\t\n118\tGiven:\n119\t  (h1) M is even unimodular\n120\t  (h2) The characteristic square identity c² ≡ σ mod 8 holds (hypothesis)\n121\tThen: 8 | σ.\n122\t\n123\tProof: By h1 (even), 0 is characteristic. By h2, 0^T M 0 ≡ σ mod 8.\n124\tBut 0^T M 0 = 0. So σ ≡ 0 mod 8.\n125\t-/\n126\ttheorem serre_even_unimodular_mod8 {n : Nat}\n127\t    (M : Matrix (Fin n) (Fin n) Int) (sigma : Int)\n128\t    (h_eu : IsEvenUnimodular M)\n129\t    (h_char_sq : CharacteristicSquareModEight M sigma) :\n130\t    8 ∣ sigma := by\n131\t  have heven := h_eu.2.2\n132\t  have hzero_char := zero_is_characteristic_of_even M heven\n133\t  have h := h_char_sq (fun _ => 0) hzero_char\n134\t  simp [selfPairing, mul_zero, zero_mul, Finset.sum_const_zero] at h\n135\t  exact Int.dvd_of_emod_eq_zero h.symm\n136\t\n137\t/--\n138\t**The algebraic bound is EXACTLY 8, not 16.**\n139\tE8 has σ = 8, which is the smallest positive value achievable.\n140\tCombined with E8Lattice.lean's verification that E8 is even unimodular\n141\twith σ = 8, this shows 8 is tight.\n142\t-/\n143\ttheorem algebraic_bound_is_8_not_16 : ¬(16 ∣ (8 : Int)) := by omega\n144\t\n145\t/-- Two copies of E8 give σ = 16 (Rokhlin's bound for smooth manifolds). -/\n146\ttheorem two_E8_gives_16 : 8 + 8 = (16 : Int) := by norm_num\n147\t\n148\t/-! ## 5. The gap between algebra and topology\n149\t\n150\tThe algebraic Serre theorem gives σ ≡ 0 mod 8.\n151\tRokhlin's theorem (topology) strengthens to σ ≡ 0 mod 16.\n152\tThe extra factor of 2 is genuinely topological — proved by:\n153\t  - Atiyah-Singer: index of Dirac operator is even (quaternionic spinors)\n154\t  - Or: Freedman's E8 manifold (topological, σ=8) shows smoothness is essential\n155\t\n156\tWe formalize the GAP between algebra and topology.\n157\t-/\n158\t\n159\t/-- The algebra-topology gap: 8 divides σ algebraically, but 16 requires topology.\n160\t    The factor of 2 between them (16/8 = 2) encodes smooth structure. -/\n161\ttheorem algebra_topology_gap :\n162\t    (16 : Nat) / 8 = 2 := by norm_num\n163\t\n164\t/-- For the full Rokhlin theorem, we would need EITHER:\n165\t    - 2 hypotheses (bordism, see HYPOTHESIS_REGISTRY spin_bordism_iso_Z)\n166\t    - OR the Atiyah-Singer index theorem (not in Mathlib)\n167\t    The algebraic Serre result + either hypothesis gives 16 | σ. -/\n168\ttheorem rokhlin_from_serre_plus_topology\n169\t    (sigma : Int)\n170\t    (h_alg : 8 ∣ sigma)           -- from Serre (proved above for even unimodular)\n171\t    (h_topo : 2 ∣ sigma / 8) :     -- the topological input: σ/8 is even\n172\t    16 ∣ sigma := by\n173\t  obtain ⟨k, hk⟩ := h_alg\n174\t  obtain ⟨m, hm⟩ := h_topo\n175\t  rw [hk, Int.mul_ediv_cancel_left _ (by norm_num : (8 : Int) ≠ 0)] at hm\n176\t  rw [hk, hm]\n177\t  exact ⟨m, by ring⟩\n178\t\n179\t/-! ## 6. Module summary -/\n180\t\n181\t/-! ## Module summary\n182\t\n183\tAlgebraicRokhlin module: the algebraic Serre theorem σ ≡ 0 mod 8.\n184\t  - IsUnimodular, IsEven, IsEvenUnimodular predicates DEFINED\n185\t  - IsCharacteristic (characteristic vector) DEFINED\n186\t  - zero_is_characteristic_of_even PROVED\n187\t  - selfPairing DEFINED\n188\t  - CharacteristicSquareModEight (key identity) as hypothesis\n189\t  - serre_even_unimodular_mod8: even unimodular → 8 | σ PROVED (conditional on char sq identity)\n190\t  - algebraic_bound_is_8_not_16: 8 is tight (E8 achieves it) PROVED\n191\t  - algebra_topology_gap: 16/8 = 2 encodes smooth structure PROVED\n192\t  - rokhlin_from_serre_plus_topology: algebraic + topological input → 16 | σ PROVED\n193\t  - Zero axioms. One hypothesis (CharacteristicSquareModEight, eliminability: hard).\n194\t  - Concrete validations on E8 and H below.\n195\t-/\n196\t/--\n197\t**Concrete validation: CharacteristicSquareModEight holds for E8.**\n198\t\n199\tE8 is even → 0 is characteristic. selfPairing(E8, 0) = 0.\n200\tσ(E8) = 8. 0 % 8 = 0 = 8 % 8. Check.\n201\t-/\n202\ttheorem char_sq_valid_e8 :\n203\t    selfPairing CartanMatrix.E₈ (fun _ => 0) % 8 = (8 : Int) % 8 := by\n204\t  simp [selfPairing, mul_zero, zero_mul, Finset.sum_const_zero]\n205\t\n206\t/--\n207\t**Concrete validation: CharacteristicSquareModEight holds for H (hyperbolic plane).**\n208\t\n209\tH is even → 0 is characteristic. selfPairing(H, 0) = 0.\n210\tσ(H) = 0. 0 % 8 = 0 = 0 % 8. Check.\n211\t-/\n212\ttheorem char_sq_valid_H :\n213\t    selfPairing hyperbolicPlane (fun _ => 0) % 8 = (0 : Int) % 8 := by\n214\t  simp [selfPairing, mul_zero, zero_mul, Finset.sum_const_zero]\n215\t-- (Module summary placeholder removed; see preceding sections.)\n216\t\n217\tend SKEFTHawking\n218\t"}]},"uuid":"dcb8898e-f982-4145-81e2-cfbfe5690b45","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:42:32.985Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"040dd983-fa73-4649-9ea4-eaf7d377ffcb","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

Use \\totaltheorems{}, \\aristotleproved{},\n12\t%% \\leanmodules{}, \\axiomcount{} instead of inline literals (NumericalFreshness\n13\t%% Gate 9 — Stage-13 fix-pass 2026-05-11).\n14\t\\input{../../docs/counts.tex}\n15\t\\input{../../docs/figuredeferred.tex}\n16\t\\newcommand{\\mathlibcommit}{\\mathlibrev}\n17\t\n18\t%% Bundle: D2 (tier 1, target PRD | JHEP)\n19\t%% Source manifest: papers/D2/source_manifest.md\n20\t%% Change log: papers/D2/change_log.md\n21\t%% Phase 7b sub-wave 7b.5 — D2 lift (skeleton + first-pass substantive draft)\n22\t%% Sources: paper7+paper8+paper9+paper10\n23\t%% L2 PRL splash already extracted from paper10 §2 content\n24\t\n25\t\\begin{document}\n26\t\n27\t\\title{Anomaly Constraints on Standard-Model Particle Content:\\\\\n28\tA Machine-Verified Four-Thread Convergence}\n29\t\n30\t\\author{John Roehm}\n31\t\\affiliation{Independent Researcher}\n32\t\\email{jgroehm@gmail.com}\n33\t\n34\t\\date{\\today}\n35\t\n36\t\\begin{abstract}\n37\tWe present a unified, machine-verified treatment of four anomaly\n38\tconstraints on Standard-Model particle content. (i) The chiral central\n39\tcharge $c_{-} = 8 N_f$ derived from sixteen left-handed Weyl\n40\tcomponents per generation, combined with the modular framing\n41\tconstraint $24 \\mid c_{-}$ from\n42\t$\\eta(\\tau{+}1) = e^{2\\pi i/24}\\eta(\\tau)$, forces\n43\t$N_f \\equiv 0 \\pmod{3}$. The minimal solution $N_f = 3$ is verified\n44\tthrough Mathlib's \\texttt{Complex.exp\\_add}; without right-handed\n45\tneutrinos $c_{-} = 15/2$ is fractional, giving an independent\n46\tgravitational-anomaly argument for $\\nu_R$. The algebraic core uses\n47\t$\\dim_{\\mathbb{F}_2} \\mathrm{Ext}^n_{A(1)}(\\mathbb{F}_2,\\mathbb{F}_2)\n48\t= 1,2,2,2,3,4$ for $n = 0,\\dots,5$ together with the change-of-rings\n49\tadjunction $\\mathrm{Hom}_A(A \\otimes_{A(1)} M, N) \\cong\n50\t\\mathrm{Hom}_{A(1)}(M, N)$. The algebraic kernel of this Ext\n51\tcomputation is formalized at the resolution + chain-complex +\n52\trank-witness level. (ii) The Z$_4$ generator\n53\t$X = 5(B-L) - 4Y$ assigns odd Z$_4$ charge to all six SM fermion\n54\tspecies; $3 \\times 15 \\equiv -3 \\pmod{16}$ in the no-$\\nu_R$ case\n55\tforces a hidden Z$_{16}$-charged sector. (iii) An integrated formal\n56\tsynthesis of the chirality wall integrates a no-go pillar\n57\t(the nine Golterman-Shamir conditions under which the\n58\tNielsen-Ninomiya obstruction applies, two-particle-fermion (TPF) evasion\n59\tvia discontinuity of \\texttt{round} at 1/2 and infinite-dimensionality\n60\tof $\\ell^2(\\mathbb{Z})$), a positive-construction pillar\n61\t(Gioia-Thorngren BdG Hamiltonian on $(\\mathbb{Z}/L)^3$ with the\n62\tcentral commutation $[H_\\text{BdG}(p),\\, q_A(p)] = 0$ closed by an\n63\tAristotle-discovered $\\sin^2 + \\cos^2 = 1$ identity), and an algebraic\n64\tpillar (Onsager Dolan-Grady, In\\\"on\\\"u-Wigner contraction\n65\t$[\\varepsilon A_m, \\varepsilon A_n] = 4\\varepsilon^2 G_{m-n}$,\n66\t$\\mathrm{su}(2)$ emanant symmetry, Witten anomaly\n67\t$\\pi_4(\\mathrm{SU}(2)) = \\mathbb{Z}_2 \\hookrightarrow\n68\t\\mathbb{Z}_{16}$). (iv) The integer 16 appears in four formally\n69\tdistinct settings (SM Weyl count, Z$_{16}$ DIII bordism index,\n70\tRokhlin's signature theorem, Kitaev DIII period-16) and the\n71\tgeneration count $N_f = 3$ appears in two independent settings\n72\t(Z$_{16}$ + modular). The end-to-end chain (from sixteen Weyl\n73\tcomponents through three formal mechanisms to one observed\n74\t$N_f = 3$) is verified by \\texttt{lake build} on a Lean 4\n75\tlibrary with \\totaltheorems{} theorems across \\leanmodules{}\n76\tmodules with \\axiomcount{} project-local axioms: the 4+1D\n77\tgapped-interface statement is carried as a tracked-hypothesis\n78\t\\texttt{Prop} (\\texttt{TPFConjecture}, formerly\n79\t\\texttt{axiom gapped\\_interface\\_axiom}, converted to a tracked\n80\t\\texttt{Prop} on 2026-05-19; corroborated in 1+1D and 2+1D by"}]},"uuid":"9957f0b5-9b2d-477b-9a97-6a2466bacdd3","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:43:29.083Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"7eff6f65-81a8-4a7f-a142-f7242e9f1bb5","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"41a4a42e-ee67-4761-8ad9-e2c87389f358","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a5ea667e8aa34be02","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Ce578kF5Ea6QjAjg1M5tG","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"I'll use section labels for the signatures section, then turn to verifying the rank-4 argument for §5.3: showing that an even unimodular indefinite 4×4 form with positive and negative signature has determinant +1, as a step toward finding a nonzero isotropic vector.\n\nThe key is a mod-4 determinant computation: since diagonal entries are even, the determinant reduces to a perfect square mod 4 (using symmetry to pair up permutation terms with single diagonal factors), ruling out det = -1 and forcing det = +1. This means the rational diagonalization has a square product of weights, making it a square-discriminant quaternary form — an important structural fact for the isotropy argument.\n\nFor the real place, indefiniteness gives mixed-sign weights, hence isotropy over ℝ, with positivity transferring from ℝ to ℚ by density. For odd primes p, unimodularity of A means its mod-p reduction is a nondegenerate rank-4 form over 𝔽_p, and Chevalley-Warning guarantees a nonzero isotropic vector there with nonvanishing gradient — a simple zero that I can use to lift via Hensel's lemma by varying a single coordinate where the gradient is nonzero.\n\nFor p = 2, I'm using reciprocity: the square-discriminant condition lets me reduce the quaternary isotropy question to a ternary form, which in turn reduces to checking a single binary Hilbert symbol.  Since I've already shown this symbol equals 1 at infinity and at every odd prime, Hilbert's product formula forces it to equal 1 at 2 as well, so the form is isotropic there too — completing the local check at every place and setting up the final local-to-global Hasse–Minkowski step.\n\nThe proof proceeds by strong induction on rank: the definite case reduces to theta series, while the indefinite case uses a primitive isotropic vector to build a hyperbolic pair, with evenness allowing the correction needed to complete the splitting.\n\nclassical E₈^a ⊕ (−E₈)^b ⊕ H^c normal form is the natural statement to invoke here, though I should be upfront that the machine proof only gets 8 | rank via induction, not the full E₈-identification of the definite part — that's the honest framing. The generator/closure lemmas (`eight_dvd_latticeSig_e8lit`, `eight_dvd_latticeSigOf_fromBlocks`, `eight_dvd_latticeSig_congr`) are exactly what would transfer 8|σ across that normal form if it were available, and they're already packaged as closure facts.\n\nI'm reconsidering how to frame §5.2: rather than calling it \"the classification route,\" I should present it as \"the reduction\" — the engine of the classification (peeling off hyperbolic planes to isolate the definite core) rather than the full normal form itself, being explicit about what's in and out of the kernel, and flagging this deviation in CONTRADICTIONS.\n\nFor §5.4 on theta functions, I'm working through the definite case: when sigPos=0 or sigNeg=0, the form is (after possibly negating) positive definite, so σ = ±rank, meaning 8|σ iff 8|rank. I'm also confirming the theta function definition by checking `latticeTheta_I_ne_zero`'s proof at τ=I, verifying that the sum is over lattice vectors with exponent πiτ(vᵀMv).\n\nNow I'm tracing through T-invariance (using evenness of vᵀMv), the S-transform via Poisson summation combined with unimodularity to show Θ(−1/τ) = (τ/i)^{d/2}Θ(τ), and then iterating (ST)³=1 around the orbit i → (i−1)/2 → i−1 → i to derive that 8 divides the rank, matching the Lean lemmas `neg_I_cpow_eq_one_iff_eight_dvd` and `eight_dvd_rank`.\n\nI want to verify that positive-definiteness plus unimodularity forces det = 1, and confirm that `latticeTheta_S` is actually proven rather than assumed in ThetaSTransform.lean, so I should grep the file to {"parentUuid":"258a8b1a-b304-4497-b945-06077aeaf8bd","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"eba3dfcd-82c5-4438-a34f-e34cfd4a6c20","agentId":"a5ea667e8aa34be02","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Qry17Cfgd6BFvcPYv77PTU","type":"tool_result","content":"114:abbrev gaussSumZ (q : V → ZMod 2) : ℤ :=\n119:theorem gaussSum_arf (q : V → ZMod 2) (hq : IsRefinement q) :\n120:    gaussSumZ q = 2 * signZ (arf q) := by\n123:  unfold gaussSumZ arf signZ\n155:def gaussSum {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] (f : ι → ZMod 2) : ℤ := ∑ x, signZ (f x)\n160:theorem gaussSum_orthogonal {ι κ : Type*} [Fintype ι] [Fintype κ]\n162:    gaussSum (fun p : ι × κ => f p.1 + g p.2) = gaussSum f * gaussSum g := by\n163:  unfold gaussSum\n169:    with the genus-1 value `gaussSum (per plane) = 2·(-1)^{Arf}`, a block-diagonal genus-`g`\n171:theorem gaussSum_pi {ι : Type*} [Fintype ι] [DecidableEq ι] {V : ι → Type*}\n173:    gaussSum (fun x : (∀ i, V i) => ∑ i, q i (x i)) = ∏ i, gaussSum (q i) := by\n174:  unfold gaussSum\n179:theorem gaussSum_eq_gaussSumZ (q : V → ZMod 2) : gaussSum q = gaussSumZ q :=\n182:/-- Genus-1, unified form: `gaussSum q = 2·(-1)^{Arf q}` for the generic Gauss sum. -/\n183:theorem gaussSum_arf' (q : V → ZMod 2) (hq : IsRefinement q) :\n184:    gaussSum q = 2 * signZ (arf q) := by\n185:  rw [gaussSum_eq_gaussSumZ]; exact gaussSum_arf q hq\n191:theorem gaussSum_genus_g {g : ℕ} (qfun : Fin g → (V → ZMod 2))\n193:    gaussSum (fun x : Fin g → V => ∑ i, qfun i (x i)) = 2 ^ g * signZ (∑ i, arf (qfun i)) := by\n194:  rw [gaussSum_pi, Finset.prod_congr rfl (fun i _ => gaussSum_arf' (qfun i) (hq i)),"}]},"uuid":"da4fac67-dc87-4ecf-a2d3-861be59af02e","timestamp":"2026-08-15T19:45:22.176Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"258a8b1a-b304-4497-b945-06077aeaf8bd","userType":"external","entrypoint":"claude-desktop","cwd":"/Users/johnroehm/Programming/PythonEnvironments/Physics/Fluid-Based-Physics-Research/SK_EFT_Hawking","sessionId":"6cf37aa2-f1c7-4e33-a712-f17c2d75d99b","version":"2.1.229","gitBranch":"HEAD","slug":"swift-singing-llama"}

{"parentUuid":"ba228ace-e9d9-4679-bad5-d30b8c8022d8","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a5ea667e8aa34be02","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011Ce57FqJsckVhB87u43MbK","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"I have read the substrate in full. Here is the section.\n\n---\n\n```latex\n\\section{Signatures of even unimodular forms}\n\\label{sec:signatures}\n\n\\subsection{The result, and what is new about the route}\n\\label{sec:sig-result}\n\nLet $M$ be a symmetric $n\\times n$ integer matrix that is\n\\emph{unimodular} ($\\det M = \\pm 1$) and \\emph{even} (every diagonal\nentry $M_{ii}$ is divisible by $2$, equivalently $b(x,x)\\in 2\\mathbb{Z}$\nfor every $x\\in\\mathbb{Z}^n$). Write $\\sigma(M)$ for its signature, the\nnumber of positive minus the number of negative eigenvalues of the real\nform $M\\otimes\\mathbb{R}$. The main result of this section is the\nfollowing.\n\n\\begin{theorem}[$8\\mid\\sigma$, unconditional]\n\\label{thm:eight-dvd}\nFor every $n$ and every even unimodular $M \\in\n\\mathrm{Mat}_{n\\times n}(\\mathbb{Z})$, $\\;8 \\mid \\sigma(M)$.\n\\end{theorem}\n\nThe statement is classical. It is van der Blij's congruence for even\nforms, and it is standard textbook material on symmetric bilinear\nforms~\\cite{vanderBlij1959,MilnorHusemoller1973}.\n% TODO: neither vanderBlij1959 nor MilnorHusemoller1973 was available to\n% the author in full text; the attribution of Theorem 1 to these sources,\n% and the characterisation of the classical proof as proceeding through\n% characteristic vectors, are unverified against the primary literature.\nNo referee needs to be told that $8$ divides the signature of an even\nunimodular form. What is new is the \\emph{route}, and the route is what\nthis section is about.\n\nThree properties distinguish it. First, it is complete inside a proof\nkernel. Theorem~\\ref{thm:eight-dvd} is the Lean~4 declaration\n\\texttt{eight\\_dvd\\_latticeSig}, whose statement is exactly the display\nabove with $\\sigma$ interpreted as the honest Sylvester signature of the\nreal form and not as a free integer parameter, and whose proof carries no\nhypothesis, no project-local axiom and no appeal to native compilation.\nTwo classical inputs were once carried as explicit hypotheses of the\nreduction, namely a local-global statement for indefinite forms and a\nmodularity statement for definite ones. Both are now theorems in the same\nlibrary, so the divisibility is unconditional rather than reduced.\n\nSecond, the route is non-circular in a sense that can be checked\nmechanically rather than argued informally. It does not compute the\nfour-dimensional spin bordism group, so it consumes no structure theory\nof the spin cobordism ring~\\cite{ABP1967}; it consumes neither Rokhlin's\ntheorem nor any statement equivalent to it; and it consumes no\ndivisibility statement about $\\sigma$ at any point. The reader can\nconfirm this from the dependency graph rather than from our word for it:\nevery declaration named in this section is reachable from the\neven-unimodularity predicate together with real quadratic-form theory,\n$p$-adic analysis and the theory of the lattice theta function, and from\nnothing else. This matters because the divisibility statements in this\narea are close enough to one another that a proof of one from another is\neasy to write down and hard to notice.\n\nThird, the argument is sharp at the boundary where sharpness is usually\nasserted rather than shown. The bound of Theorem~\\ref{thm:eight-dvd}\ncannot be improved to $16$: the $E_8$ form is even unimodular with\n$\\sigma = 8$, and both facts are machine-checked\n(\\texttt{e8lit\\_even\\_unimodular} and \\texttt{e8lit\\_latticeSig}). Every\nstrengthening to $16 \\mid \\sigma$ in the literature is therefore a\nstatement about $4$-manifolds and not about forms, and any formalisation\nof such a strengthening must expose the extra input.\nSection~\\ref{sec:sig-residual} isolates that input, and\nSection~\\ref{sec:sig-arf} closes off the most natural attempt to remove\nit.\n\n\\paragraph{What this does and does not do for the rest of the paper.}\nTheorem~\\ref{thm:eight-dvd} does \\emph{not} enter the derivation of\n$N_f \\in 3\\mathbb{Z}$. That derivation runs entirely through the chiral\ncentral charge and the modular framing constraint, and it would stand\nunchanged if this section were deleted. Nor does\nTheorem~\\ref{thm:eight-dvd} enter the $\\mathbb{Z}_{16}$ argument of\nSection~\\ref{sec:z16-drinfeld}. The result is a second, independent\ncontribution of the present work, unconditional where the generation\nconstraint is conditional on a proposal of another author. It is\npresented here because it is the unconditional mathematics in this paper\nand because the integer $8$ it produces is the same integer that governs\nthe signature of the intersection form of a spin $4$-manifold, which is\nthe object the anomaly discussion of Sections~\\ref{sec:modular}\nand~\\ref{sec:z16-drinfeld} is ultimately about. The connection is one of\nsubject matter, not of logical dependence, and we do not claim more.\n\n\\subsection{The signature calculus, and the reduction}\n\\label{sec:sig-calculus}\n\n\\paragraph{The signature, grounded.}\nA formalisation of a signature theorem is only as good as its signature.\nWe define\n\\begin{equation}\n\\sigma(M) \\;:=\\; \\mathrm{sig}^{+}\\!\\left(M\\otimes\\mathbb{R}\\right)\n\\;-\\; \\mathrm{sig}^{-}\\!\\left(M\\otimes\\mathbb{R}\\right),\n\\label{eq:latticesig}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathrm{sig}^{\\pm}$ are the positive and negative inertia indices\nof the real quadratic form $x \\mapsto x^{\\mathsf T} M x$ on\n$\\mathbb{R}^n$, taken from the general theory of Sylvester's law of\ninertia rather than introduced for this purpose\n(\\texttt{LatticeSignature.lean}). Two consequences are used below and\nboth are proved from~\\eqref{eq:latticesig} directly: orientation reversal\n$\\sigma(-M) = -\\sigma(M)$, and the rank bound $|\\sigma(M)| \\leq n$. When\n$M\\otimes\\mathbb{R}$ is positive definite, $\\sigma(M) = n$; when it is\nnegative definite, $\\sigma(M) = -n$.\n\n\\paragraph{The calculus.}\nThree structural laws of $\\sigma$ carry the argument, and each is a\nseparate machine-checked statement.\n\n\\emph{Congruence invariance.} For $P$ with $\\det P$ a unit in\n$\\mathbb{Z}$,\n\\begin{equation}\n\\sigma\\!\\left(P^{\\mathsf T} M P\\right) \\;=\\; \\sigma(M)\n\\label{eq:sylvester}\n\\end{equation}\n(\\texttt{latticeSig\\_congr}). This is Sylvester's law transported from\n$\\mathbb{R}$ to $\\mathbb{Z}$-congruence, and it is what allows a\nsignature computed in one basis to be quoted in another. A companion\nstatement gives the same invariance under relabelling of the index type,\nwhich is needed because a block decomposition produces a sum type rather\nthan $\\mathrm{Fin}\\,n$.\n\n\\emph{Block additivity.} For nondegenerate blocks $A$ and $B$,\n\\begin{equation}\n\\sigma(A \\oplus B) \\;=\\; \\sigma(A) + \\sigma(B),\n\\label{eq:blockadd}\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $A\\oplus B$ is the block-diagonal matrix\n(\\texttt{latticeSigOf\\_fromBlocks}). Nondegeneracy is not decoration.\nThe inertia indices of a degenerate form do not add across a direct sum\nin the way~\\eqref{eq:blockadd} asserts, because the radical is\ndistributed between the summands; the hypothesis is discharged for each\nblock that occurs here from unimodularity.\n\n\\emph{Generator values.} $\\sigma(E_8) = 8$, $\\sigma(-E_8) = -8$ and\n$\\sigma(H) = 0$, where $H = \\left(\\begin{smallmatrix} 0 & 1 \\\\ 1 & 0\n\\end{smallmatrix}\\right)$ is the hyperbolic plane. The last two follow\nfrom the first by orientation reversal and by direct computation. The\nfirst is the only analytically nontrivial evaluation in this section,\nbecause a signature is a count of eigenvalue signs and therefore a\nstatement about $\\mathbb{R}$. We obtain it from an integral certificate\nrather than from an eigenvalue computation. There is an explicit integer\nmatrix $C$, whose columns are twice the $E_8$ simple roots, with\n\\begin{equation}\nC^{\\mathsf T} C \\;=\\; 4\\,E_8 .\n\\label{eq:cholesky}\n\\end{equation}\nBoth sides of~\\eqref{eq:cholesky} are products of small integers, so the\nidentity reduces in the kernel by evaluation (\\texttt{c8\\_eq}). Cast to\n$\\mathbb{R}$ it exhibits $4E_8$ as a Gram matrix; $C$ is invertible\nbecause $\\det(C)^2 = \\det(4E_8) = 4^8\\det(E_8) \\neq 0$, which uses\nunimodularity and evaluates no $8\\times 8$ determinant; hence $4E_8$ and\nthen $E_8$ are positive definite, and $\\sigma(E_8) = 8$ follows from the\ndefinite case of~\\eqref{eq:latticesig}. The doubling in~\\eqref{eq:cholesky}\nis what makes the certificate integral: the $E_8$ root lattice has a\nhalf-integer basis, so the naive $LDL^{\\mathsf T}$ pivots are rational and\na rational certificate does not reduce.\n\nUnimodularity and evenness of $E_8$ are established on the same explicit\nmatrix by exhibiting the integer inverse and checking\n$E_8 \\cdot E_8^{-1} = \\mathbf{1}$, which is a few hundred small integer\nmultiplications, rather than by expanding the determinant\n(\\texttt{e8lit\\_mul\\_inv}, \\texttt{e8lit\\_even\\_unimodular}).\n\n\\paragraph{The reduction.}\nThe classical route to Theorem~\\ref{thm:eight-dvd} is the classification\nof even unimodular lattices: every such $L$ is isomorphic to\n\\begin{equation}\nE_8^{\\,\\oplus a} \\;\\oplus\\; (-E_8)^{\\,\\oplus b} \\;\\oplus\\; H^{\\,\\oplus c},\n\\qquad \\sigma(L) = 8(a-b),\n\\label{eq:classification}\n\\end{equation}\nafter which $8\\mid\\sigma$ is immediate. The calculus above is exactly\nwhat is needed to make~\\eqref{eq:classification} yield the conclusion in\na kernel: each generator satisfies $8\\mid\\sigma$,\n\\eqref{eq:blockadd} propagates the divisibility across the direct sum, and\n\\eqref{eq:sylvester} transports it back along the isomorphism. Those\nclosure facts are shipped (\\texttt{RokhlinClassification.lean}).\n\nThe proof we actually give does not use the normal\nform~\\eqref{eq:classification}. It uses the recursion that produces it,\nwhich is weaker and therefore cheaper, and it is stated as follows.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Reduction to two classical inputs]\n\\label{prop:reduction}\nSuppose\n\\begin{itemize}\n\\item[{\\rm[HM]}] every indefinite even unimodular integer form admits a\nprimitive isotropic vector, and\n\\item[{\\rm[$\\Theta$]}] every definite even unimodular integer form\nsatisfies $8\\mid\\sigma$.\n\\end{itemize}\nThen $8\\mid\\sigma(M)$ for every even unimodular integer form $M$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\nThe proof is strong induction on the rank\n(\\texttt{eight\\_dvd\\_latticeSig\\_of\\_HM\\_of\\_Theta}). If $M$ is definite,\nmeaning $\\mathrm{sig}^{+} = 0$ or $\\mathrm{sig}^{-} = 0$, apply\n[$\\Theta$]. Otherwise $M$ is indefinite and [HM] supplies a primitive\n$v$ with $v^{\\mathsf T} M v = 0$. Primitivity and unimodularity give a\n$w$ with $v^{\\mathsf T} M w = 1$, and evenness upgrades $w$ to a genuine\nhyperbolic partner: writing $w^{\\mathsf T} M w = 2k$, which is possible\nprecisely because the form is even, the vector $w' = w - k v$ satisfies\n\\begin{equation}\nv^{\\mathsf T} M v = 0, \\qquad v^{\\mathsf T} M w' = 1, \\qquad\nw'^{\\mathsf T} M w' = 0\n\\label{eq:hyperbolic-pair}\n\\end{equation}\n(\\texttt{exists\\_hyperbolic\\_pair}). The plane spanned by $v$ and $w'$ is\na copy of $H$ and splits off orthogonally. Passing to the Gram matrix\n$M'$ of the orthogonal complement, one has\n\\begin{equation}\n\\sigma(M) = \\sigma(M')\n\\label{eq:split}\n\\end{equation}\nbecause $\\sigma(H) = 0$ (\\texttt{latticeSig\\_split}), and $M'$ is again\neven unimodular, of rank $n-2$\n(\\texttt{residGram\\_evenUnimodular}). The induction hypothesis applies.\n\nThe recursion terminates at the definite forms and at rank $0$, and the\nsmall ranks it passes through are not vacuous, so [HM] is needed at every\nrank the recursion can reach. This is the point at which a reduction\nbecomes a proof, and Sections~\\ref{sec:sig-indefinite}\nand~\\ref{sec:sig-definite} discharge the two inputs.\n\n\\paragraph{Scope of the classification claim.}\nWe are claiming Proposition~\\ref{prop:reduction} together with the\ndischarge of [HM] and [$\\Theta$], not the normal\nform~\\eqref{eq:classification} itself. The existence statement in\n\\eqref{eq:classification} is not in our library. Its\nsignature-zero case is (a form all of whose blocks are hyperbolic\nplanes), but the identification of the definite part with copies of\n$E_8$ is not; the definite branch below stops at the divisibility\n$8 \\mid \\mathrm{rank}$, which is all that $8\\mid\\sigma$ requires. A\nreader who wants~\\eqref{eq:classification} will not find it here, and we\ndo not use it.\n\n\\subsection{The indefinite branch, and rank four}\n\\label{sec:sig-indefinite}\n\n\\begin{proposition}[{[HM]} discharged at every rank]\n\\label{prop:hm}\nEvery indefinite even unimodular integer form admits a nonzero integer\nisotropic vector, and hence a primitive one.\n\\end{proposition}\n\nThe upgrade from nonzero to primitive is content extraction: divide by\nthe greatest common divisor of the coordinates, which does not disturb\nisotropy (\\texttt{exists\\_primitive\\_isotropic\\_of\\_isotropic}). The\nsubstance is the nonzero statement (\\texttt{hasWeakIsotropicVector}), and\nit splits by rank.\n\n\\emph{Ranks one and three do not occur.} A $1\\times 1$ even unimodular\nform would have $M_{00}$ simultaneously even and equal to $\\pm 1$. For a\n$3\\times 3$ symmetric matrix with even diagonal, every term of the\nLeibniz expansion is even, so $2 \\mid \\det M$, contradicting\nunimodularity (\\texttt{not\\_evenUnimodular\\_one},\n\\texttt{not\\_evenUnimodular\\_three}). These are the rank-$1$ and rank-$3$\ninstances of ``even unimodular implies even rank'', proved directly so as\nto avoid characteristic-two Pfaffian machinery, and they are elementary\nin the strict sense that they use no divisibility property of $\\sigma$.\n\n\\emph{Rank two is explicit.} With both diagonal entries even,\n$\\det M = M_{00}M_{11} - M_{01}^2 \\equiv -M_{01}^2 \\pmod 4$, which lies\nin $\\{0,3\\}$, so $\\det M = 1$ is impossible and $\\det M = -1$\n(\\texttt{det\\_eq\\_neg\\_one\\_of\\_evenUnimodular\\_two}). The vector\n$(1 - M_{01},\\, M_{00})$, or $(1,0)$ when $M_{00} = 0$, is then isotropic\nby direct computation, since its value is $M_{00}(1 + \\det M)$. Note that\nno real-signature input is used: every even unimodular binary form is\nindefinite.\n\n\\emph{Rank at least five is generic.} At rank $\\geq 5$ a diagonal form\nover $\\mathbb{Q}_p$ is isotropic for every $p$, with no hypothesis beyond\nnonvanishing coefficients, and over $\\mathbb{R}$ isotropy follows from\nindefiniteness. The local-global spine then applies\n(\\texttt{diag\\_indefinite\\_rat\\_zero},\n\\texttt{weakIsotropic\\_of\\_five\\_le}). Even unimodularity is not required\nat this rank. Two pieces of plumbing make this usable on a Gram matrix\nrather than a diagonal form. The form is diagonalised over $\\mathbb{Q}$\nand the congruence is exhibited explicitly so that it can be cast to every\ncompletion; and indefiniteness, which is stated over $\\mathbb{R}$, is\ntransferred to $\\mathbb{Q}$ by density, since a real vector on which the\nform is positive lies in an open set and therefore contains a rational\npoint (\\texttt{sigPos\\_cast\\_pos}, \\texttt{sigNeg\\_cast\\_pos}).\n\n\\emph{Rank four is the frontier.} It is the only rank at which local\nisotropy can genuinely fail: anisotropic quaternary forms exist over every\n$\\mathbb{Q}_p$, so nothing is automatic and the even unimodular structure\nmust be used. We give the argument in full.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Rank-four weak isotropy]\n\\label{prop:rank4}\nEvery indefinite even unimodular $4\\times 4$ integer form $A$ admits a\nnonzero integer vector $v$ with $v^{\\mathsf T} A v = 0$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\n\\emph{Step 1: the discriminant is a square.} We first show\n$\\det A = +1$, excluding the shapes with $\\det A = -1$ and\n$\\sigma = \\pm 2$. Write the diagonal entries as $2g_i$ and the\noff-diagonal ones as $a = A_{01}$, $b = A_{02}$, $c = A_{03}$,\n$d = A_{12}$, $e = A_{13}$, $f = A_{23}$. Reduce the Leibniz expansion\nmodulo $4$. Every monomial containing at least two diagonal factors is\ndivisible by $4$ outright. Every monomial containing exactly one diagonal\nfactor corresponds to a permutation with exactly one fixed point, hence to\na $3$-cycle; the $3$-cycle and its inverse contribute the same product\nwith the same sign, so those monomials pair off and their sum acquires the\nmissing second factor of $2$. What survives is\n\\begin{equation}\n\\det A \\;\\equiv\\; \\left(af - be + cd\\right)^2 \\pmod 4\n\\label{eq:det4}\n\\end{equation}\n(\\texttt{det4\\_evenDiag\\_sq}). A square modulo $4$ lies in $\\{0,1\\}$, and\n$-1 \\equiv 3$ does not, so unimodularity forces $\\det A = 1$\n(\\texttt{det\\_eq\\_one\\_of\\_evenUnimodular\\_four}).\n\nNow diagonalise over $\\mathbb{Q}$, writing $A = P^{\\mathsf T}\n\\mathrm{diag}(w)\\, P$ with $\\det P$ a unit. Taking determinants,\n$1 = \\det(P)^2 \\prod_i w_i$, so\n\\begin{equation}\n\\prod_{i} w_i \\;=\\; \\left(\\det P\\right)^{-2}\n\\label{eq:sqdisc}\n\\end{equation}\nis a square (\\texttt{isSquare\\_prod\\_weights}). Clearing denominators\ncoordinatewise, the integral diagonal form $\\sum_i d_i x_i^2$ with\n$d_i = \\mathrm{num}(w_i)\\cdot\\mathrm{den}(w_i)$ also has square\ndiscriminant. This is the structural payoff of Step~1, and it is what\nmakes the rest of the argument possible: a quaternary form of square\ndiscriminant is controlled by a \\emph{single} binary Hilbert symbol,\nwhereas a general quaternary form is not.\n\n\\emph{Step 2: the real place.} Indefiniteness gives a positive and a\nnegative coefficient among the $d_i$, and a two-variable combination of\nthe corresponding coordinates is isotropic over $\\mathbb{R}$\n(\\texttt{diag\\_real\\_isotropic\\_of\\_signs}).\n\n\\emph{Step 3: odd primes, by Chevalley--Warning and Hensel.} Fix an odd\nprime $p$. Since $\\det A = 1$ is a $p$-adic unit, $A$ is\n$\\mathbb{Z}_p$-unimodular, and its reduction $\\bar A$ modulo $p$ is a\nrank-four form over $\\mathbb{F}_p$ with $\\det\\bar A \\neq 0$. A quadratic\nform in at least three variables over a finite field has a nontrivial\nzero, because the number of solutions of a polynomial system whose total\ndegree is smaller than the number of variables is divisible by the\ncharacteristic, and the trivial solution is one of them; this is the\nChevalley--Warning theorem, and it is available for an arbitrary finite\nfield and any rank at least three\n(\\texttt{finite\\_field\\_form\\_isotropic}). Let $\\bar x \\neq 0$ be such a\nzero.\n\nThe zero is \\emph{simple}. Its gradient is $\\bar A\\bar x$, and if that\nvanished then $\\bar x$ would lie in the kernel of $\\bar A$, contradicting\n$\\det \\bar A \\neq 0$. Choose a coordinate $j$ with\n$(\\bar A \\bar x)_j \\neq 0$, lift $\\bar x$ arbitrarily to $x_0$ over\n$\\mathbb{Z}_p$, and vary only coordinate $j$. Setting $y$ equal to $x_0$\nwith its $j$-th coordinate zeroed, the form becomes a univariate quadratic\nin that coordinate:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\left(y + t\\,e_j\\right)^{\\mathsf T}\\! A \\left(y + t\\,e_j\\right)\n= A_{jj}\\,t^2 + 2\\,(Ay)_j\\, t + y^{\\mathsf T} A y ,\n\\label{eq:univariate}\n\\end{equation}\nthe cross terms coinciding by symmetry of $A$\n(\\texttt{mulVec\\_update\\_coord\\_quadratic}). Call this polynomial $F$.\nAt $t = a := (x_0)_j$ one has $F(a) = x_0^{\\mathsf T} A x_0$, which is\ndivisible by $p$ by construction, and\n$F'(a) = 2\\,(A x_0)_j$, which is a $p$-adic unit because $p$ is odd and\n$(\\bar A\\bar x)_j \\neq 0$. Hence $|F(a)|_p < |F'(a)|_p^2 = 1$, and\nHensel's lemma produces an exact zero $z \\in \\mathbb{Z}_p$ of $F$. The\nvector $y + z\\,e_j$ is a nonzero $\\mathbb{Z}_p$-isotropic vector for $A$,\nand therefore a $\\mathbb{Q}_p$-isotropic one\n(\\texttt{isotropic\\_padicInt\\_of\\_unit\\_det}). Transporting through the\nexplicit congruence of Step~1 gives isotropy of the diagonal form at every\nodd $p$.\n\n\\emph{Step 4: the prime two, by reciprocity.} No local computation at\n$p = 2$ is performed. Instead, the square-discriminant hypothesis\nconverts local isotropy at a place $v$ into the vanishing of a single\nbinary Hilbert symbol. Concretely, if $abcd = s^2$ with all entries\nnonzero, then over any field\n\\begin{equation}\na x^2 + b y^2 - c z^2 - d w^2 = 0\n\\;\\;\\Longleftrightarrow\\;\\;\nz^2 = -ab\\,x^2 + ac\\,y^2\n\\label{eq:quaternary-ternary}\n\\end{equation}\nhas a nontrivial solution (\\texttt{quaternary\\_sqdisc\\_iso\\_iff\\_ternary}),\nthe right-hand side being the norm form of the quaternion algebra attached\nto the pair $(\\alpha,\\beta) = (-ab,\\,ac)$. Isotropy at $v$ is thus\nequivalent to $(\\alpha,\\beta)_v = 1$. Steps~2 and~3 give\n$(\\alpha,\\beta)_\\infty = 1$ and $(\\alpha,\\beta)_p = 1$ for every odd\nprime. Hilbert's product formula\n\\begin{equation}\n\\prod_{v} \\left(\\alpha,\\beta\\right)_v \\;=\\; 1 ,\n\\label{eq:hilbert-product}\n\\end{equation}\nthe product running over the real place and all primes and being finitely\nsupported, then forces the remaining factor to be $1$\n(\\texttt{hilbertPrime\\_two\\_eq\\_one\\_of\\_real\\_odd}). The place $2$, which\nis the hard place for quadratic forms, is obtained for free from the\nothers. We emphasise that~\\eqref{eq:hilbert-product} is itself a theorem\nin our library and not an assumption: it is assembled from the\nbimultiplicativity of the local symbols, quadratic reciprocity and the\nsupplementary laws (\\texttt{hilbertGlobalProd\\_eq\\_one}).\n\n\\emph{Step 5: local to global.} With every place discharged, the\nsquare-discriminant quaternary Hasse--Minkowski statement applies. It\nroutes through the ternary form on the right of\n\\eqref{eq:quaternary-ternary} and requires neither weak approximation nor\nDirichlet's theorem on primes in arithmetic progressions\n(\\texttt{quaternary\\_sqdisc\\_solvable\\_of\\_local\\_no\\_two},\n\\texttt{diag\\_four\\_solvable\\_of\\_local\\_no\\_two}). The resulting\nnontrivial rational zero is cleared of denominators to an integral one\n(\\texttt{exists\\_int\\_isotropic\\_of\\_rat}), and transported back through\nthe congruence of Step~1 to $A$ itself. This proves\nProposition~\\ref{prop:rank4} (\\texttt{weakIsotropic\\_rank\\_four}).\n\nTwo remarks on this argument. First, it is the even unimodular hypothesis,\nand nothing else, that supplies each local input: unimodularity gives the\n$\\mathbb{Z}_p$-unit determinant that Step~3 needs, and evenness gives\nthe mod-$4$ congruence~\\eqref{eq:det4} that Step~1 needs. Second, the\nargument is confined to rank four by design. At rank at least five the\nlocal inputs are automatic and the even unimodular structure is not used\nat all; at rank four it is used twice, and both uses are essential.\n\n\\subsection{The definite branch, by theta modularity}\n\\label{sec:sig-definite}\n\nFor a definite form, $\\sigma = \\pm\\,\\mathrm{rank}$, so $8\\mid\\sigma$ is\nequivalent to $8\\mid\\mathrm{rank}$. The bridge from the inertia\nhypothesis to definiteness is short. A unimodular form is nondegenerate,\nso its radical is trivial; with $\\mathrm{sig}^{-} = 0$, the identity\n$\\mathrm{sig}^{+} + \\mathrm{sig}^{-} + \\dim(\\mathrm{radical}) =\n\\mathrm{rank}$ forces $\\mathrm{sig}^{+}$ to be the full dimension, and a\nform whose maximal positive subspace is everything is positive definite.\nThe case $\\mathrm{sig}^{+} = 0$ is the mirror image, handled by negating\nthe form (\\texttt{eight\\_dvd\\_latticeSig\\_of\\_definite}). So the content\nis the following.\n\n\\begin{proposition}[Modular weight]\n\\label{prop:theta}\nA positive-definite even unimodular integer form of rank $d$ has\n$8 \\mid d$.\n\\end{proposition}\n\nThe proof is the classical theta argument, carried out analytically. For\na real positive-definite $G$ set\n\\begin{equation}\n\\Theta_G(\\tau) \\;=\\; \\sum_{v \\in \\mathbb{Z}^d}\ne^{\\,\\pi i \\tau\\, v^{\\mathsf T} G v},\n\\qquad \\mathrm{Im}\\,\\tau > 0 ,\n\\label{eq:theta}\n\\end{equation}\nwhich converges absolutely (\\texttt{latticeTheta}).\n\n\\emph{$T$-invariance.} If $G$ comes from an even integer form then\n$v^{\\mathsf T} G v \\in 2\\mathbb{Z}$ for every $v$, so each summand\nin~\\eqref{eq:theta} is unchanged under $\\tau \\mapsto \\tau + 1$ and\n\\begin{equation}\n\\Theta_G(\\tau + 1) = \\Theta_G(\\tau)\n\\label{eq:theta-T}\n\\end{equation}\ntermwise, with no summability argument required\n(\\texttt{latticeTheta\\_T\\_int}).\n\n\\emph{$S$-transformation.} Multivariate Poisson summation applied to the\nGaussian $v \\mapsto e^{\\pi i \\tau v^{\\mathsf T} G v}$ gives the\nfunctional equation relating $\\Theta_G$ to the theta function of the dual\nform $G^{-1}$ (\\texttt{latticeTheta\\_S}). Two facts collapse it. A\npositive-definite unimodular form has $\\det = +1$, which removes the\n$(\\det G)^{-1/2}$ prefactor; and for an integral $A$ with unit\ndeterminant, $A^{-1}$ is again integral and\n$A^{-1} = (A^{-1})^{\\mathsf T} A\\, A^{-1}$, so the dual lattice is the\noriginal lattice in a different unimodular basis and its theta function is\nliterally the same function (\\texttt{latticeTheta\\_inv\\_eq}). What remains\nis the self-transformation\n\\begin{equation}\n\\Theta_G\\!\\left(-1/\\tau\\right)\n\\;=\\; \\left(\\tau/i\\right)^{d/2}\\,\\Theta_G(\\tau).\n\\label{eq:theta-S}\n\\end{equation}\n\n\\emph{The multiplier is forced.} Composing~\\eqref{eq:theta-S}\nwith~\\eqref{eq:theta-T} gives the combined step\n$\\Theta_G(-1/(w+1)) = ((w+1)/i)^{d/2}\\,\\Theta_G(w)$\n(\\texttt{theta\\_ST}). Iterating it three times traverses the orbit\n\\begin{equation}\ni \\;\\longmapsto\\; \\tfrac{i-1}{2} \\;\\longmapsto\\; i-1\n\\;\\longmapsto\\; i ,\n\\label{eq:st-orbit}\n\\end{equation}\nwhich closes because $(ST)^3$ is the identity in the modular group. The\naccumulated multiplier must therefore be $1$. It can be divided out\nbecause $\\Theta_G(i)$ is a sum of positive reals and so is nonzero\n(\\texttt{latticeTheta\\_I\\_ne\\_zero}), and the three factors combine to a\nsingle power because each base has positive real part, keeping the\nprincipal branch of the complex power additive\n(\\texttt{mul\\_cpow\\_of\\_re\\_pos}). The product of the three multipliers\nalong~\\eqref{eq:st-orbit} is $(-i)^{d/2}$, so\n\\begin{equation}\n(-i)^{d/2} = e^{-i\\pi d/4} = 1\n\\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n8 \\mid d\n\\label{eq:eight-dvd-rank}\n\\end{equation}\n(\\texttt{neg\\_I\\_cpow\\_eq\\_one\\_iff\\_eight\\_dvd},\n\\texttt{eight\\_dvd\\_rank}). This proves Proposition~\\ref{prop:theta}.\n\nProposition~\\ref{prop:reduction} applied to Propositions~\\ref{prop:hm}\nand~\\ref{prop:theta} proves Theorem~\\ref{thm:eight-dvd}.\n\nIt is worth recording what the two branches cost. The definite branch is\nanalysis: Poisson summation, absolute convergence of a Gaussian lattice\nsum, and the principal branch of $z^{s}$. The indefinite branch is\narithmetic: finite fields, $\\mathbb{Z}_p$, Hensel lifting, and quadratic\nreciprocity in the form of the Hilbert product formula. Neither branch\nuses the other, and neither uses any topology.\n\n\\subsection{The residual factor of two, and why it is irreducible}\n\\label{sec:sig-residual}\n\nThe signature of the intersection form of a closed smooth spin\n$4$-manifold is divisible by $16$, not merely by\n$8$~\\cite{Rokhlin1952}.\n% TODO: Rokhlin1952 (Doklady Akad. Nauk SSSR 84, 221) was not available to\n% the author; the statement of Rokhlin's theorem as quoted here is taken\n% from its standard formulation and is unverified against the original.\nThe gap between Theorem~\\ref{thm:eight-dvd} and that statement is exactly\na factor of two, and the purpose of this subsection is to say precisely\nwhere the factor of two comes from and to prove that it cannot come from\nthe form.\n\nWe record the interface first. A closed smooth spin $4$-manifold\ncontributes two pieces of data to the argument and no others: its\nintersection form on $H^2(M;\\mathbb{Z})$, which is even by Wu's formula\nand unimodular by Poincar\\'e duality, and the divisibility\n$2 \\mid \\sigma/8$. The Lean structure \\texttt{SmoothSpinManifold4}\ncarries exactly these, with its signature \\emph{defined} as\n$\\sigma(\\text{form})$ in the sense of~\\eqref{eq:latticesig} rather than\nsupplied as a free parameter. On that interface,\n\\begin{equation}\n8 \\mid \\sigma(M)\n\\label{eq:interface-eight}\n\\end{equation}\nis a derived theorem and not a carried field\n(\\texttt{SmoothSpinManifold4.eight\\_dvd\\_sig}); it is\nTheorem~\\ref{thm:eight-dvd} applied to the intersection form. Composing\nit with the carried divisibility gives\n\\begin{equation}\n8 \\mid \\sigma \\;\\wedge\\; 2 \\mid \\sigma/8\n\\;\\;\\Longrightarrow\\;\\; 16 \\mid \\sigma\n\\label{eq:compose}\n\\end{equation}\n(\\texttt{SmoothSpinManifold4.rokhlin}). We state plainly that this second\nstatement is conditional on the interface field $2\\mid\\sigma/8$, and that\nit must be: $16\\mid\\sigma$ is false for general even unimodular forms, as\n$E_8$ witnesses. A formalisation that presented $16\\mid\\sigma$ as\nunconditional at the level of forms would be proving something untrue.\n\n\\paragraph{What the residual factor is.}\nThe input $2\\mid\\sigma/8$ is the evenness of the $\\hat A$-genus. For a\nclosed spin $4$-manifold the index of the Dirac operator equals\n$-\\sigma/8$, and in dimension four that index is even because the spinor\nbundle carries a quaternionic structure commuting with the Dirac\noperator, so its kernel and cokernel are quaternionic vector spaces.\nEquivalently, and more geometrically, it is the vanishing of the Arf\ninvariant of the quadratic refinement carried by a smoothly embedded\ncharacteristic surface, in the congruence of\nFreedman--Kirby~\\cite{FreedmanKirby1978},\n\\begin{equation}\n\\sigma(M) \\;\\equiv\\; \\Sigma\\cdot\\Sigma \\;+\\; 8\\,\\mathrm{Arf}(M,\\Sigma)\n\\pmod{16},\n\\label{eq:freedman-kirby}\n\\end{equation}\nin which $\\Sigma \\subset M$ is a smoothly embedded surface representing a\ncharacteristic class and $\\mathrm{Arf}(M,\\Sigma)$ is the Arf invariant of\na quadratic refinement on $H_1(\\Sigma;\\mathbb{Z}/2)$. For a spin manifold\nthe class $0$ is characteristic, both correction terms drop out, and\n\\eqref{eq:freedman-kirby} reduces to $16\\mid\\sigma$.\n% TODO: neither the Atiyah--Singer identification of the index with\n% -sigma/8 and its quaternionic parity, nor the Freedman--Kirby\n% congruence (5.14), could be checked against a primary source available\n% to the author; both are stated from their standard formulations and are\n% unverified.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it is irreducible.}\nThere are two statements here, of increasing strength, and we prove the\nfirst and cite the second.\n\nThe algebraic statement is that no property of the intersection form can\nsupply the factor of two, and it follows from\nTheorem~\\ref{thm:eight-dvd} being sharp. The implication ``$M$ even\nunimodular $\\Rightarrow 16 \\mid \\sigma(M)$'' is false, refuted by $E_8$,\nwhich is even unimodular with $\\sigma = 8$; both halves of that\nrefutation are machine-checked. Consequently any correct derivation of\n$16\\mid\\sigma$ must consume an input that is not a function of the\nisomorphism class of the form. The interface above is honest precisely\nbecause it exposes such an input rather than deriving it, and the previous\nsubsections show that everything else on the path is derivable.\n\nThe stronger statement is that the input cannot be supplied by the\ntopology of the underlying manifold either, but requires the smooth\nstructure. Freedman's classification of simply-connected closed\ntopological $4$-manifolds produces a closed simply-connected\n\\emph{topological} $4$-manifold whose intersection form is\n$E_8$~\\cite{Freedman1982}.\n% TODO: Freedman (1982) was not available to the author; the existence and\n% properties of the topological E8 manifold are stated from their standard\n% formulation and are unverified against the primary source.\nIts form is even, so it is spin in the sense available to a topological\nmanifold, and its signature is $8$. It therefore violates the conclusion\n$16\\mid\\sigma$ while satisfying every hypothesis that does not mention\nsmoothness. Smoothness is what the Dirac operator needs in order to exist,\nand it is what a smooth embedding of the characteristic surface\nin~\\eqref{eq:freedman-kirby} needs in order to be available. This is not\na defect of our formalisation to be repaired later. It is the theorem's\nactual content, and the interface is built so that the content is visible.\n\n\\subsection{The lattice Arf invariant cannot be the residual factor}\n\\label{sec:sig-arf}\n\nThe most natural attempt to remove the residual input is to look for it in\nthe mod-two structure of the lattice itself. An even lattice $L$ carries a\ncanonical quadratic refinement of its mod-two intersection pairing,\n\\begin{equation}\n\\bar q : L/2L \\longrightarrow \\mathbb{Z}/2,\n\\qquad\n\\bar q(x) \\;=\\; \\tfrac{1}{2}\\,b(x,x) \\bmod 2 ,\n\\label{eq:redquad}\n\\end{equation}\nwhich is well defined precisely because $L$ is even\n(\\texttt{redQuad}). Since $\\bar q$ is a quadratic refinement of a\nnondegenerate symplectic form over $\\mathbb{F}_2$, it has an Arf\ninvariant, readable off the sign of its Gauss sum through the genus-$g$\nidentity\n\\begin{equation}\n\\sum_{x} (-1)^{\\bar q(x)} \\;=\\; 2^{\\,g}\\,(-1)^{\\mathrm{Arf}(\\bar q)} ,\n\\label{eq:gauss-arf}\n\\end{equation}\nwhich is itself machine-checked (\\texttt{gaussSum\\_genus\\_g}). Write\n$\\mathrm{Arf}(M)$ for the invariant so defined\n(\\texttt{arfOfForm}). The candidate bridge is\n\\begin{equation}\n\\sigma(M)/8 \\;\\equiv\\; \\mathrm{Arf}(M) \\pmod 2\n\\qquad\\text{for every even unimodular } M ,\n\\label{eq:arf-bridge}\n\\end{equation}\nwhich would replace the geometric input of\nSection~\\ref{sec:sig-residual} by a finite computation on $L/2L$. It is\nattractive: it has the right shape, it is finite, and it is the lattice\nshadow of the genuinely correct geometric congruence\n\\eqref{eq:freedman-kirby}. It is also false.\n\n\\begin{theorem}[The lattice Arf bridge is refuted]\n\\label{thm:arf-nogo}\nStatement~\\eqref{eq:arf-bridge} is false. Explicitly, $E_8$ is even\nunimodular with $\\sigma(E_8)/8 = 1$ and $\\mathrm{Arf}(E_8) = 0$.\n\\end{theorem}\n\nThe witness is computed rather than argued\n(\\texttt{lattice\\_arf\\_bridge\\_refuted}). On $E_8/2E_8$, which has\n$2^8 = 256$ elements,\n\\begin{equation}\n\\sum_{x \\in E_8/2E_8} (-1)^{\\bar q(x)} \\;=\\; +16 \\;=\\; +2^4 ,\n\\label{eq:e8-gauss}\n\\end{equation}\nevaluated by kernel reduction and not by native compilation\n(\\texttt{gaussSum\\_redQuad\\_e8lit\\_eq}). By~\\eqref{eq:gauss-arf} with\n$g=4$ the positive sign is the $\\mathrm{Arf} = 0$ branch, so\n$\\mathrm{Arf}(E_8) = 0$, while $\\sigma(E_8)/8 = 1$ is odd. The Gauss sum\nalso decomposes as $136 - 120$, and the count is recognisable: the $120$\nclasses on which $\\bar q = 1$ are the $240$ roots of $E_8$ taken in\n$\\pm$ pairs. Thus~\\eqref{eq:arf-bridge} would read $1 \\equiv 0$.\n\nTheorem~\\ref{thm:arf-nogo} is not an isolated numerical accident, and\nthree structural facts show that the bridge cannot be repaired inside the\nlattice category.\n\n\\emph{The lattice Arf invariant is constant.} Each generator of the even\nunimodular family has vanishing Arf invariant\n(\\texttt{arfOfForm\\_e8lit\\_eq\\_zero},\n\\texttt{arfOfForm\\_neg\\_e8lit\\_eq\\_zero},\n\\texttt{arfOfForm\\_hyp\\_eq\\_zero}), and the Gauss sum is multiplicative\nunder orthogonal direct sums with every factor on the positive branch, so\n$\\mathrm{Arf}$ is additive and vanishes identically on even unimodular\nforms. A constant function cannot equal $\\sigma/8 \\bmod 2$, which is not\nconstant.\n\n\\emph{It is orientation-blind.} Every coefficient of $\\bar q$ is fixed\nmodulo two under negation of the form, because $-a \\equiv a$ and\n$-(2k)/2 = -k \\equiv k$, so $\\bar q(-M) = \\bar q(M)$ and hence\n$\\mathrm{Arf}(-M) = \\mathrm{Arf}(M)$\n(\\texttt{redQuad\\_neg\\_eq}, \\texttt{arfOfForm\\_neg\\_eq}). But\n$\\sigma(-M) = -\\sigma(M)$. No invariant derived from $\\bar q$ can see the\nsign of the signature, and $E_8$ against $-E_8$ makes that concrete: the\nArf invariants agree while the signatures are $8$ and $-8$.\n\n\\emph{The discriminant form is trivial.} For a unimodular lattice the\ndiscriminant group $L^{*}/L$ vanishes. The entire finite quadratic-form\napparatus that produces signature congruences from Gauss sums therefore\nhas nothing to act on beyond the mod-two reduction, and it can certify\nonly $\\sigma \\equiv 0 \\pmod 8$, which is Theorem~\\ref{thm:eight-dvd} and\nwhich we already have by other means. It is blind to $\\sigma$ modulo\n$16$. The refinement of $\\bar q$ to values in $\\mathbb{Z}/4$ does not\nhelp, since on an even lattice $b(x,x)$ takes only even values and the\nrefinement collapses back to the same $\\pm 1$ sum.\n\nThe distinction that~\\eqref{eq:arf-bridge} elides is between two\ndifferent objects that share a name. The Arf invariant appearing in the\ncorrect congruence~\\eqref{eq:freedman-kirby} lives on\n$H_1(\\Sigma;\\mathbb{Z}/2)$ for a smoothly embedded surface $\\Sigma$ in a\n$4$-manifold; the one appearing in~\\eqref{eq:arf-bridge} lives on\n$L/2L$ for the intersection lattice. The first depends on a smooth\nembedding and the second does not, and the data determining the first is\nnot present in, and not recoverable from, the lattice.\nTheorem~\\ref{thm:arf-nogo} is the precise statement that the substitution\nis unavailable, and it explains why the interface of\nSection~\\ref{sec:sig-residual} must keep $2\\mid\\sigma/8$ as an input.\n\nThe Gauss-sum machinery itself is correct and reusable: it proves\n\\eqref{eq:gauss-arf} and it computes $\\mathrm{Arf}$ on any even form\npresented in symplectic normal form. What it does not do, and provably\ncannot do, is certify the factor of two.\n```\n\n---\n\n## CONTRADICTIONS BETWEEN THE BRIEF AND THE SOURCES\n\nSix items. The first three are substantive and change what the section is allowed to say.\n\n**1. §5.2's requested framing is not what the machine proof does.** The brief asks for \"The classification route: even unimodular ⟹ L ≅ E8^a ⊕ (−E8)^b ⊕ H^c, whence σ = 8(a−b)\" as the proof. The library's own docstring in `SpinRokhlinInterface.lean` (lines 33–40) says the opposite:\n\n> \"⚠ The full classification EXISTENCE statement (every even unimodular form ≅ `E₈^a ⊕ (−E₈)^b ⊕ H^c`) is NOT itself in-tree: the σ = 0 case IS (`HyperbolicNormalForm.exists_hyperbolic_congr`, pure-H blocks), but the definite branch stops at the `8 ∣ rank` DIVISIBILITY — the E₈-identification of the definite part … is the open K8b interior brick.\"\n\nThe actual proof (`VanDerBlijReduction.eight_dvd_latticeSig_of_HM_of_Theta`) is strong induction on rank: definite forms go to [Θ]; indefinite forms peel one hyperbolic plane via [HM] and recurse. It never constructs the normal form. `RokhlinClassification.lean` ships the generator/block/congruence *closure* facts that would transfer `8∣σ` across the normal form if one had it, but they are not what the capstone calls. I wrote §5.2 as the reduction, presented the normal form as the classical route it corresponds to, and added an explicit \"Scope of the classification claim\" paragraph disclosing that the existence statement is not in-tree. **If the lead wants the normal-form framing restored, it would be an overclaim and I decline to write it.**\n\n**2. The E8 Cartan-matrix lemmas are `native_decide`, so they are not kernel-pure, and they are not on the main chain.** The brief says of `e8_det_one`, `e8_diagonal_all_two`, `e8_diagonal_even`: \"These are genuine and use Mathlib's E8 Cartan matrix.\" Both halves are true, but `E8Lattice.lean:42-63` proves them by `native_decide` (the file header says so: \"All proofs by native_decide or decide\"). That puts `Lean.ofReduceBool` in their axiom set, which fails the repo's own kernel-purity bar. Separately, `E8Literal.lean` explains that they were superseded precisely for this reason, and the theorems that actually feed Theorem 1 are about the *literal* matrix `E8lit`, proved kernel-pure by inverse exhibition and by the `C8ᵀC8 = 4·E8lit` certificate. **There is no in-tree theorem `E8lit = CartanMatrix.E₈`** (I grepped; zero matches), so the Cartan lemmas are not linked to the chain at all. I therefore did not cite `e8_det_one` / `e8_diagonal_all_two` / `e8_diagonal_even` anywhere, and §5.2 attributes the E8 evaluation to `e8lit_mul_inv`, `e8lit_even_unimodular`, `c8_eq` and `e8lit_latticeSig`. Citing the Cartan lemmas beside a kernel-purity claim would be a false statement.\n\n**3. Not one primary source this section characterizes is in the cache, and D2's bibliography has none of them.** I checked `Lit-Search/Phase-*/primary-sources/` at the workspace root (not repo-relative) and `papers/D2/paper_draft.tex`'s `thebibliography`. Absent from both: van der Blij, Milnor--Husemoller, Freedman 1982, Freedman--Kirby, Guillou--Marin, Atiyah--Singer, Serre. `Rokhlin1952` and `ABP1967` have bibitems but no cached text. In particular I argue the *algebraic* irreducibility of the residual factor internally, from E8 being even unimodular with σ = 8, and use Freedman's manifold only for the strictly stronger smooth-versus-topological claim, so a failure to retrieve Freedman would cost one paragraph rather than the argument. **The brief forbids `% TODO`; the drafter's read-first rule outranks it and requires the marker over the unsourced claim.** Four new bibitems are needed (`vanderBlij1959`, `MilnorHusemoller1973`, `Freedman1982`, `FreedmanKirby1978`) and I did not invent their bibliographic data.\n\n**4. I dropped Finashin--Kharlamov--Viro entirely.** The old §2.4 (line 368) says the route \"isolates the irreducible content exactly as Finashin--Kharlamov--Viro describe.\" No cached source, no bibitem, no Lean docstring mentions it, and I could not determine what claim of theirs is being invoked. Guillou--Marin appears in the brief's eponym list but I used only Freedman--Kirby.** The Lean docstrings pair them (\"geometric Guillou–Marin characteristic-surface Arf-vanishing\"), but I have no source for either and adding a second unverifiable attribution buys nothing. One TODO now covers the congruence.\n\n**6. One phrase in a Lean docstring is wrong and I did not reproduce it.** `RokhlinArfNoGo.lean:43` states \"(`E₈`'s *characteristic-surface* Arf is `1`; its *lattice* L/2L Arf is `0`)\". For a spin manifold the zero class is characteristic and the surface term vanishes, which is exactly how (5.14) reduces to 16 | σ; and Freedman's E8 manifold is not smooth, so it has no smoothly embedded characteristic surface to carry an Arf invariant. The docstring's *point* (the two Arf invariants are different objects) is right and I kept it; the parenthetical numeric claim is not, and I wrote the reduction correctly instead.\n\n**Confirmations, for completeness.** Every Lean statement the brief quoted is verbatim correct against the source, and every theorem the brief flagged as weak is weak exactly as described: `e8_sigma_div_8` is `(8:ℤ) ∣ 8`; `rokhlin_gap` is `16 ∣ 2*8 ∧ ¬(16 ∣ 8)`; `char_sq_valid_e8` evaluates `selfPairing` at the zero vector.
